- **2026-06-17 — Sprint 006: model-backed Producer seam (real Responder, deterministic-testable).** Lifted the stub's "emits note=kind" ceiling: a Producer with `model: true` (+ `prompt`) now calls the runtime's REAL `Responder.respond(prompt)` and emits its kind carrying the response. `build_from_spec(spec, responder=None)` defaults to the runtime's `DeterministicResponder` (CI mode — pure, seeded, no network, replay-stable); `server.py` `_responder_for(spec)` selects `deterministic` (default) | `ollama` (`OllamaResponder`, real LLM, loud failure if absent — never a silent fake). This is the runtime's own pattern (topologies written against `Responder`; the mode is which Responder the run is handed). A model Producer is declared `deterministic` iff its Responder is. `builder.py` + `server.py`. Dual contract: artifact (2 files; ruff clean) + observation (`test_build_model_producer_runs_the_responder`: a model topology built with DeterministicResponder → the emitted payload `note` equals `DeterministicResponder(seed=0).respond("rate this")`, NOT "rater" — the responder genuinely ran). 22 server tests pass. Rubber Duck clean (below). The Studio authoring UI for `model`/`prompt`/responder is sprint 007 (front-end, two-track).

- **2026-06-17 — Sprint 007: Studio authoring for model-backed Producers.** Exposed the sprint-006 seam in the Studio: a `model` checkbox + a `prompt` field per Producer row, and a responder `<select>` (deterministic (CI · pure) default | ollama (real LLM)) by the build action. `buildSpec()` emits `producer.model`/`prompt` + `spec.responder` — exactly what `build_from_spec`/`_responder_for` accept. `web/studio.html` + `web/studio.js`. Dual contract: artifact (2 files; node --check clean; 22 server tests unaffected) + observation BOTH tracks: structural (`e2e_studio.js` — every row has a model checkbox; a responder selector present; marking judge model + prompt → build → the run's Verdict carries the Responder's output `stub[0]:…`, NOT "judge" — a model-backed run that executed) AND perceptual (frames 35/35b captured AND VIEWED — model checkbox + prompt per row, responder selector by the build, legible, on-vocabulary). Rubber Duck clean (below). **The E2 "full parity" Studio is now functionally complete: visual (canvas) + structured (form) authoring of Producers (stub or model) / Views / Triggers / Routes / composed Termination + responder → real validate + build → view.** NEXT: send the drag-canvas + model-Producer batch (sprints 005–007) to the duplex-pipe reviewer (Architect's request).

- **2026-06-17 — Model-backed Producers use the runtime's OWN Responder (no fakes).** A model Producer calls `substrate.reference`'s real `Responder`: `DeterministicResponder` (CI mode — pure, seeded; the DEFAULT, so authored model topologies replay byte-identically and need no network) or `OllamaResponder` (real LLM, selected at build; FAILS LOUD as ProducerFailed if Ollama is absent — never a silent stub). Reuse the runtime's truth rather than reinvent a fake. Ratified by review #42 ("REAL and HONEST").

- **2026-06-17 — Studio build sequence + discipline (binding direction, this session).** Build the E2 full-parity Studio in this order: build seam under test (001) → static topology view (002) → form-first authoring (003) → Routes + any_of/all_of composition (004) → drag-canvas (005) → model-backed Producer seam (006) → model authoring UI (007). **Discipline (now mandatory):** every front-end increment runs BOTH observation tracks — structural E2E AND perceptual capture-AND-VIEW — before "done"; sprint card with declared contracts before code; the eight-word vocabulary binds (no own signals vocabulary — the UI reads substrate's v0.2). - **2026-06-17 Claude Code (Opus 4.8, 1M)** — COMPREHENSION_AFFIRMATION (recorded as substrate-ui comes under formal kit discipline). This project is **substrate-ui**, the read + thin-control console over the **Substrate** runtime. The UI emits NO signals of its own, so it locks no vocabulary; the contract that binds it is the eight-word tone canon (Producer, Bus, View, Predicate, Trigger, Route, TerminationPolicy, Topology — no anthropomorphism, no marketing reframes), verified clean by a standing grep. **The kit loop here:** from the Studio increment forward, each increment gets a sprint card with a declared dual + observation contract BEFORE code; build → `test_server.py` (real server/real api over HTTP) → live `e2e_console.js` (real Chrome) → independent review via the duplex-pipe reviewer → fold. **The hard rule binding me:** halt-and-articulate, and never present looks-done-but-inert as done — review #39 caught exactly that (a live Studio seam with zero tests because no card forced its artifact contract); the retrofit closes it.

- **`unfired_triggers` false-positive on matured-but-input-build-failed Triggers (review #41, LATENT).** `/api/build` derives "fired" from the trigger_ids present on `run_graph` instances. But a Trigger whose Predicate matured yet whose `input_builder` raised/returned non-canonical output emits `substrate.InputBuildFailed` with NO `TriggerFired` and NO instance (`sequencer.py`: input_builder runs before TriggerFired is emitted) — so `unfired_triggers` would list it as "never fired" when it actually fired-but-failed-to-build-input, conflating two states the Studio will want to show differently. **NOT reachable today:** the stub's `input_builder` is `lambda ctx: ctx.event.payload` (already-canonical, can't raise). **Re-visit condition:** BEFORE real-model Producers / custom input_builders land — then derive "fired" from the `TriggerFired`/`InputBuildFailed` events keyed by trigger_id (not from run_graph instances), or segment the signal into "unreachable predicate" vs "fired-but-failed-input".

The tab appears ONLY when a renderable shape exists (hidden for `code_review` etc.). `web/app.js` (`findGrids`/`renderScene`/`updateScene` + the `gvScene` toggle + `STATE.scene`) + `web/index.html` (the `#gvScene` tab + `.scene-grid`/`.cell` CSS). NO backend change. Dual contract: artifact (`node --check web/app.js` clean; eight-word grep clean — scene/cell/grid are not primitive names; 24 server tests unaffected) + observation BOTH tracks — **structural** (`capture_scene.js`, real Chrome: scene tab visible for `game_of_life` + hidden for `code_review`; clicking renders a 5×5 grid = 25 cells with 3 live; scrubbing to each `Generation` seq changes the live-cell positions) AND **perceptual** (3 frames captured to `screenshots/scene_gol_gen{0,1,2}.png` AND VIEWED: the blinker renders as a 3-cell green bar and oscillates **vertical → horizontal → vertical** as the cursor steps seq 3 → 132 → 261; layout clean, on-vocabulary, no overflow). Sprint card declared the dual + observation contract BEFORE code (`sprints/sprint-008-scene-panel.md`). Rubber Duck Pass clean (## Sprint tail). PNGs still written for the polish look (two-track: decode = mechanical half, look = vision-model-judge half). Verified: `npm run e2e` PASS incl. the scene section; `npm run capture:scene` PASS (gen0 vertical / gen1 horizontal / gen2 vertical — every decoded grid matches the record); game_of_life tests 2 passed + the guard; node --check + eight-word grep clean. - **2026-06-22 — Run-as-graph legibility: split each lane into queued | ran (Architect-flagged).** The firing-anchored lane bar (`fired_seq` -> `ended_seq`) with the spawn dot at `started_seq` read as "spawned at the end of a solid green bar" — nonsensical. **DIAGNOSED by correlating `run_graph` with the screenshot** (the SDD discipline — read the state, don't guess): game_of_life cells fire early (5,7,9…) but start LATE (55,58,61…) and run ~2 seqs (ended 57,60,63…), so `started_seq` sits at ~96% of the fired->ended bar — i.e. 94% of the bar was the cell WAITING in the single-writer admission queue, ~4% the actual run. **FIXED:** each lane now renders two segments — a faint hatched `fired->started` (scheduled/queued) + a solid status-coloured `started->ended` (ran) — with the spawn dot at the boundary (the run START). Legend relabeled (scheduled/queued · ran->completed · started). `web/app.js` renderGraph + `web/index.html` (.qbar/.leg-q CSS). Verified BOTH tracks on game_of_life as known ground truth: **structural/mechanical** — a Playwright correlation read each rendered lane's qbar/bar/dot geometry and asserted it against `run_graph` fired/started/ended: **53/53 lanes correlate, 0 mismatches, dot==run-start for every started lane** (gated in `e2e_console.js` §2) — AND **perceptual** (`screenshots/graph_segments.png` VIEWED: faint-queued + solid-ran + dot-at-boundary; the staircase now legibly "each cell queued progressively longer, ran briefly"; world lanes mostly-solid). - **2026-06-22 — Drift-watchlist fold: replay loop → requestAnimationFrame (render-rate decoupled).** The play loop was `setInterval(_tick)` calling a full `render()` per seq, so high speeds capped at render cost (~60/s) — render-bound (the sprint 009 drift item). Now a `requestAnimationFrame` loop advances the cursor by (elapsed × speed) seqs and renders ONCE per frame, so the replay RATE is decoupled from the ~60 fps render rate (480/s advances ~8 seqs/frame, one render). Restored honest higher speeds (selector now **4/30/120/480 seq/s**, default 30; the review #50 relabel to a 60-max is superseded — these are now genuinely attainable, not fiction). `web/app.js` (the `_frame`/`startPlay`/`stopPlay` rAF rewrite; `setInterval`→`requestAnimationFrame`) + `web/index.html` (speed options; dropped the "render-bound near the top" title). NO backend change. Both tracks: **structural** (`e2e_console.js` §14 now plays a 200-seq TAIL at 480/s and reaches max 263==263 in a 1.5 s window the old ~60/s loop physically could NOT clear — proving the decoupling, not just stop-at-end; rewind lands near 0; pause holds) + **perceptual** (`screenshots/replay_raf.png` VIEWED: ⏮ ⏸ green-while-playing + slider mid-replay; speed options confirmed 4/30/120/480). - **2026-06-22 — Sprint 012 (item A): glider fixture closes the pixel-decode mirror-blind spot — sprint 012 CLOSED.** Review #50 noted `capture_scene`'s pixel decode catches transpose but NOT a pure L-R/U-D mirror render bug, because the blinker is mirror-symmetric (maps to itself under mirroring). Added (substrate `20b70cd`) a `game_of_life_glider` bundled topology — an ASYMMETRIC, MOVING glider (7×7, generations=4). `capture_scene` now decodes BOTH the blinker (V/H/V) AND the glider (5 generations, seqs 3→999), and asserts each glider frame's decoded grid != its own L-R mirror — so a mirror render bug WOULD diverge here (it can't on the symmetric blinker). Refactored the decoder into `openScene`/`decodeFrame` helpers. **Found + fixed a real harness race**: `openScene` read `STATE.scene` before the record finished loading (`selectRecord` sets `STATE.name` at :193 but `STATE.scene` at :208), so it got the PREVIOUS record's seqs — fixed by fetching the ground-truth Generation seqs from the record API + waiting until `STATE.events` matches the record's max seq. Both tracks: structural (`npm run capture:scene` PIXEL-DECODE PASS: blinker 3 gens + glider 5 gens all decode-match; `glider_mirror_sensitive=true` every frame) + perceptual (`screenshots/scene_glider_gen0.png` VIEWED: the textbook glider, asymmetric + clean). Closes the mirror-blind Drift item — **sprint 012 (B rAF, C grouping, C2 prune, A glider) DONE**. Only `noted` drift item left: D (fold capture_scene into the canonical harnesses).

- **2026-08-03 — Sprint 017 (W2.2): the delegate child as a navigable branch in the parent's flow — CLOSED.** delegate (substrate W2.1) records the child as its OWN run and folds `{answer, child_root, steps}` into the parent's `ToolResult`; the UI showed that as one opaque line. Now the I/O surface renders it as a navigable branch. **Pressure-test first (in the card):** not already solved (no in-record linking / inner-root handling existed), and the real gap — a cockpit `/api/agent` run records to served `runs/` but its tool workspace is `~/.substrate/sessions/`, so real delegate children land UNSERVED. **The stitch, keeping the read surface `runs/`-only:** `server.py` `_resolve_child_name(child_root)` MATCHES the ToolResult's `child_root` path against the served set (never READS the arbitrary path) and `/api/resolve_child` returns the served name or null; `web/app.js` `renderIO` renders `↳ delegated child: <name>` (a cyan link → `selectRecord`) when servable, else display-only `↳ … recorded at <path>` (never a dead link), plus a `◂ parent` breadcrumb that returns; `gen_demo_records.py` generates a deterministic `demo_delegate` parent (a scripted `delegate` tool carrying the child's absolute record path) + `demo_delegate_child` (a real calculator run), both under `runs/`. **Observation contract, both tracks:** structural — `harness/e2e_delegate.js` (gated: `npm run e2e:delegate`, added to `ci.yml`) drives real Chrome: select `demo_delegate` → I/O → assert the `.branch[data-child='demo_delegate_child']` → click → land in the child (its `FinalAnswer`) → assert the `◂ parent` crumb → click back. PASS. perceptual — `harness/capture_delegate.js` element-screenshots the bounded I/O pane; both frames VIEWED: the parent shows `↳ delegated child: demo_delegate_child` (cyan link), the child shows `◂ parent: demo_delegate` + its own calculator record — legible, on-theme, tone canon holds (lowercase, no emoji). **Dual contract:** `node --check web/app.js` clean; 30 server tests pass (server.py touched); all four e2e green together (e2e / e2e:studio / e2e:assay / e2e:delegate). **Rubber Duck Pass:** *Sequence* — parent I/O lists the ToolResult artifact; `_resolve_child_name` matches `child_root`→`demo_delegate_child`; the branch link fires `selectRecord` + sets `delegateParent`; the child view renders the crumb; the crumb clears `delegateParent` + reselects the parent. *Observations (six):* missing pair — none; order — the crumb only shows after a branch navigation (rail picks clear it); vocabulary gap — none (reads substrate's kinds, no UI vocab); payload anomaly — the ToolResult artifact still gists as `output=[object Object]` (a pre-existing `gistPayload` limit for object payloads; the branch below it carries the meaning — noted, not W2.2's scope); timing surprise — none; tone trace — copy is lowercase textual markers, on-canon. *Disposition:* one surfaced (the `[object Object]` gist — a `## Drift watchlist` candidate), rest clean. **Honest limit:** the demo child is servable so the branch is navigable; a REAL cockpit delegate child lands in the session workspace (unserved) and renders display-only until live cockpit-delegation writes children into the served dir — the named follow-on. **NEXT:** live cockpit delegation (delegate in the `/api/agent` suite + child records under a served dir), or substrate phase W3 (MCP).

- **2026-08-04 — W2.2 FOLLOW-ON: a live cockpit delegation is navigable (the display-only limit closed).** Sprint 017 proved the delegated-child branch on a committed demo but left real cockpit children display-only (they landed in the session workspace, unserved). Closed now: substrate `make_delegate` gained a `child_record_root(n) -> Path` hook (redirects the child RECORD; the workspace stays under the delegation dir); the cockpit `_agent` composes `delegate` into the real-model suites (ollama/cli) with `child_record_root=lambda n: RUNS/f"{base}_c{n}.record"`, so child records land as flat SERVED `runs/` records. **Live-verified (kimi-k2.6:cloud):** a real cockpit agent delegated "compute (2+3)*4"; the parent's ToolResult `child_root` = `runs/delegate_child_3e366fe6_c0.record` (served), `/api/resolve_child` returned its name, the child record is served + in the rail index (2 ToolCalls add/mul + FinalAnswer). So the delegated-child branch is navigable for a REAL run, not just the demo. **W2 phase gate MET:** an agent handed a splittable task delegates, the child is recorded + served + citable, and the flow shows the navigable branch. **Dual contract:** substrate 10 delegate tests (incl. `test_child_record_root_redirects_...`) + full suite; 30 server tests pass (server `_agent` refactored to extract the responder + compose delegate); all four e2e green. delegate substrate-side change committed in the substrate repo; the cockpit wiring here.

Emitter enforcement is namespace-split: values in the `substrate.*` namespace must be in the closed set (catches typos like `substrate.RunStartedd`); other-namespace values need only be non-empty strings (application kinds like CodeChunk, ToolCall, Critique that substrate's vocab doesn't enumerate). `inspectProducer`'s "unknown" fallback drops instead of emits. Four gates: parity green (44/44 emits), build green, parent e2e green (after the initial closed-set-too-tight fault surfaced and got fixed to the namespace-split shape), signals grader green (325 signals, 37/37 in-order, all pairings pass).   7. `ENTITY_MERGE_PROPOSED: AgentRun ↔ Run` (the substrate Run whose topology is a tool-loop). Evidence: same review; the rationale doc's `AgentRun` entity IS a substrate Run with a specific topology shape.
  8. `ENTITY_MERGE_PROPOSED: Event ↔ Envelope`. Evidence: same review; the `event` the user clicks in `EVENT_INSPECTED{seq, kind, ...}` IS the substrate Envelope with fields `seq`, `kind`, `payload`.

- **2026-08-29 — Sprint 037b CLOSED (via CLOSEOUT-ADDENDUM). Session perceptual + signal captures live; three real UX/state-management bugs surfaced and fixed same commit.** Two new harnesses: `capture_session.js` writes four screenshots to `screenshots/37-*.png` (terminal-view-empty, terminal-view-mid-turn, desktop-view-mid-session, desktop-view-four-columns); `capture_session_signals.js` writes 23-signal fixture to `captures/sprint-037/session.jsonl` and `grade:session-signals` runs `capture-grade.ts --kind session` (4 invariants PASS: bookends, contains-in-order, VIEW_SWITCHED closed-set+desktop-render pairing, driver-session bookends). **The harness earned its keep — perceptual capture caught THREE real bugs fifteen prior JS harnesses missed:** (1) `mountTerminal`'s `terminal-column` class set `display:flex` unconditionally, overriding `.view{display:none}` so BOTH views rendered simultaneously — fix: CSS gate `.view.active .terminal-column`; (2) desktop-view chrome (pickers, `+ new session`, studio, verdict, diffsel, resumebtn) rendered in the shared `.head` and bled into terminal-view — violated PRODUCT-SPEC §13 View A ("just the agent terminal, filling the window") — fix: `desktop-only` class on each + `:has()` CSS rule `.head:has(#view-toggle.on-terminal) .desktop-only{display:none !important}`; (3) class-wipe regressions exposed by the CSS fix — `mountWorkspaceShapeBadge` and `renderVerdict` were assigning `className =` and wiping inherited classes — fix: both switched to `classList.add`/`classList.remove` to preserve `desktop-only`. **Fourth grader-side gap surfaced by the session fixture's flip round-trip:** `_toggleView` did NOT call `render()` on flip-in to desktop, so `checkViewSwitched`'s "desktop container remounts its inner pane on flip-in" invariant sat vacuous across every single-flip fixture — session fixture's terminal→desktop→terminal round-trip was the first to expose it. Fix: `_toggleView` now calls `render()` on flip-in when a record is selected. Grader passed on the second run. **Card deviations recorded in `sprint-037b-CLOSEOUT-ADDENDUM.md`:** Rule-6 stretch acknowledged — two harness files + package.json edits + three code fixes (`terminal.ts` CSS, `app.ts` renderVerdict + `_toggleView`) + `index.html` head edits. Audit trail preserved because every fix carries a comment naming the perceptual capture as the finder. New npm scripts wired into signals chain (`capture:session`, `capture:session-signals`, `grade:session-signals`). Gates: check:vocab-parity 69 tags (58 live + 11 retired); check:tsc-new clean; check:ui-parity 10/10; full `npm run signals` chain PASS across SEVENTEEN JS fixtures + 10 pytest parity cases + 1 session-jsonl grader. Cleared: **sprint 035x** (slash-router extraction — no dependencies) + **sprint 038** (piece-G review fold). Piece-G queue at close: 037a + 037b closed; remaining = 035x → 038. Sprint 037b CLOSEOUT at `sprints/sprint-037b-CLOSEOUT-ADDENDUM.md`.

Piece-G scope-closure: PRODUCT-SPEC §13 View A satisfied end-to-end; §13 View B satisfied via 036a-f; §9c workspace immutability enforced at terminal + desktop; §2a slash inventory shipped (17 slashes extracted at 035x); tech spec §10 satisfied both halves; parity gate (036f) proves daemon contract deterministic across UI + CLI. **Piece G done.** Review doc at `process/reviews/REVIEW-2026-08-29-piece-g-fold.md`.

- **2026-08-29 Claude — HALT `architectural_miss`: piece G is invalid, will be rolled back. Post-mortem at `process/planning/POSTMORTEM-2026-08-29-piece-g-architectural-miss.md`.** Every sprint from 033 onward was built against a wrong decomposition. The Architect's actual ask: "move the CLI terminal to its own page, and make it into the Session thing" — refined during post-mortem to "not even move the terminal, but simply update it to reflect the new session reality." What shipped: a two-view scaffold, a "+ new session" button + dialog, five desktop-view "session controls," an eleven-tag dock retirement, and a "Piece G closed" claim at sprint 038. The 036 series is a spec-invention (nothing in the product spec says session controls belong on the desktop view). 037c destroyed a dock the correct architecture keeps. 038's self-review rubber-stamped it because the correctness lens was absent — only SDD discipline was checked. **Everything from 033 onward rolls back or reassesses.** Priority is NOT redesign. Priority is: existing dock terminal, updated to expose the additional session_topology knobs (workspace, isolate, tools) alongside the driver + params it already has. Same shape as the CLI's `substrate chat --workspace X --isolate --tools A,B` flags. Reorg/redesign becomes a SEPARATE later effort. Awaiting Architect ruling on rollback scope + sequence.
  - Cards that need to roll back or reassess: 033 (two-view scaffold), 034b (rail rewrite — reassess), 035 (session-turn wiring — keep the wire, rehome from #view-terminal to #termdock), 035s/t/u/v/w (extended chrome — salvage widgets, rehome mounts), 035x (slash-extraction pattern — keep pattern, target file changes), 036a-f (DELETE the entire family), 037a/b (DELETE — built against wrong architecture), 037c (REVERT — restore #termdock + the eleven retired vocab tags), 038 (INVALIDATE the "closed" claim), 040a/b (reassess post-rollback), 040c typing pass (KEEP — orthogonal to the miss).
  - Vocab: v0.7.3 retirement was destructive under the correct architecture. A new lock will restore the eleven dock-tied tags (or resurrect the pre-037c state). Pending Architect direction on how.
  - Discipline gates kept: pre-commit hook (`.githooks/pre-commit`), `tsc --noEmit` in `npm run build`, zero-tsc baseline. These caught nothing about this miss — they catch code regressions, not architectural ones — but they stay.
  - Key lesson (kit-level, filed at `KIT_DIARY.md`): sprint cards decompose FROM the product spec. When a card's premise is spec-invented, the whole downstream chain inherits the invention. Reviews measured SDD discipline but not correctness-of-scope. There is no gate that checks "does this card match what the product spec asks for" before dispatch. That gate now needs to exist.

#### Sprint 044 (2026-08-31, closed) — piece G close: the terminal drives a real cloud model
- **Scope:** the last unfinished stripe of piece G. Every wired-up part — the driver picker, all nineteen slashes, Ctrl+C → `/api/session/<id>/interrupt`, the SSE renderer with `ModelReply` in accent, the /model PATCH — landed in sprints 035-041. What remained: on a fresh open the terminal seeded `deterministic` every time, so the daily driver was demonstrably driving nothing.
- **Fixes (four edits + one new harness):**
  - `web/app.ts:877` — drop `driverDefault: "deterministic"` from the `mountTerminal(#view-terminal, …)` call so the terminal reads the server's default instead.
  - `web/terminal.ts` — empty-sentinel fallback for `driverName`; also read a `?driver=<name>` URL query so a harness that must not pay cloud tokens (the sprint 035 lifecycle test) pins deterministic without a server env or restart.
  - `web/terminal.ts:175` (`_openSession`) — when neither `h.driverName` nor a visibly-bound picker has a value, fetch `/api/models` default. `pickerSelect?.value` is treated as authoritative only when its parent is `offsetParent !== null` (visible), because the desktop picker is `display: none` pre-session and its first option is the literal `"deterministic"` (driver_picker.ts:52). Reading it otherwise would seed deterministic on every first open — the exact bug this sprint fixes.
  - `server.py:_agent_models` — refresh the `prefer` list against live Ollama Cloud on this box: `kimi-k2.6` → `kimi-k2.7-code`, `glm-5.1` → `glm-5.2`. Verified via `/api/tags` + a hi-in-three-words `/api/chat` probe (1.4s round-trip).
  - `harness/capture_terminal_daily_driver.js` — new. Real backend, real cloud model, no mocks. Flip to terminal view, type a real prompt, assert `DRIVER_SESSION_STARTED{driver_name != "deterministic"}`, wait for a `.accent` row in `#terminal-body`, assert non-empty text, `/exit`, assert `DRIVER_SESSION_ENDED`. Three screenshots at named anchors (44-terminal-session-opened.png, 44-terminal-model-reply-rendered.png, 44-terminal-session-ended.png).
  - `harness/capture_terminal_session.js` — one line: `page.goto(`${BASE}/?driver=deterministic`)` keeps the sprint 035 lifecycle test cost-neutral now that the daily-driver default is a paid cloud model.
- **Dual contract:** artifact — `npm run build` clean (tsc --noEmit + vite build) at 121ms; typecheck clean. Observation — both harnesses green live: the new daily-driver capture rendered "hello" from `kimi-k2.7-code:cloud` in the terminal body; the sprint 035 lifecycle test still passes on deterministic via the URL pin.
- **Live run receipt (2026-08-31, this box):**
  - `ok  session started: driver=kimi-k2.7-code:cloud tokens=262144`
  - `ok  ModelReply rendered: "hello"`
  - `ok  PARK_LANDED — turn 1 complete`
  - `ok  DRIVER_SESSION_ENDED fired on /exit`
- **Piece G status:** closed. Type into the terminal, real model responds, the toggle button flips to the desktop side which already works. The minimum to use it as a daily driver is here.

#### Sprint 045 (2026-08-31, closed) — terminal toolbar correct from the start (three commits)
- **Scope:** eight small edits landing across three commits (`45fe436`, `8261810`, `137be05`). Each earned its keep separately; each ties to a specific user complaint. See KIT_DIARY findings 32-36.
- **Driver picker visible pre-session** (`driver_picker.ts`): was `display:none` pre-session, then `pickerSelect?.value` was empty on first open and the terminal fell through to "deterministic." Now stays visible with the `/api/models` default pre-selected so the picker acts as the create-time choice too.
- **`think` defaults ON** (`server.py`): kimi-k2.7-code, glm-5.2, nemotron-3-super all produce measurably better answers with `think=True`. `/set think off` mid-session for the exceptions.
- **Tools drawer dropped from the header** (`terminal.ts`): sessions run unrestricted by default; `/tools <comma-list>` remains for mid-session restriction. The comma input read as required and nobody used it.
- **Live session rows clickable** (`rail.ts`): `_mkSession` had no onclick. Now `div.onclick = () => selectRecord(s.session_id)` — server's `_record_path` routes `s_<id>` to `~/.substrate/sessions/<id>/record`, so clicking a live row loads its graph/events/summary like any regular record.
- **Terminal is the default view** (`state.ts` + `app.ts`): opening the app lands on the terminal directly (`?view=desktop` still lands on the record browser for the fifteen Playwright harnesses that assert `#view-desktop.active` on load).
- **Bottom-dock terminal on desktop view** (`index.html` + `app.ts` `_placeTerminal`): terminal DOM mounted once into a movable `.terminal-column`, re-parented between `#view-terminal` (fullscreen) and `#terminal-dock` (bottom of the desktop view) on toggle. Same DOM, so session + SSE + input survive the flip.
- **Rail refreshes on session-changed**: the `substrate:session-changed` handler now also calls `_rail.refresh()`. New/patched sessions surface in the "live sessions" bucket without a page reload.

#### Sprint 046 (2026-08-31, closed) — session workspace matches product spec (per-session, mkdir'd)
- **Scope:** commit `72fe073`. Diagnostic on live session `s_8c86dca9869b41ab80cc0b3a`: the bash tool raised `FileNotFoundError` before running any command. Root cause: `server.py:1059` defaulted `workspace` to `~/.substrate/sessions/default/` (a shared singleton) and nothing ever created it. `subprocess.run(cwd=<nonexistent>)` raises before executing.
- **Product spec drift:** round 12 §3 line 35 + §9c line 587 name the default as `~/.substrate/sessions/<id>/workspace/` — PER-session, substrate creates it. The shared `default` folder was never part of the design.
- **Fix:** mint the session_id early when no `--workspace` is provided, default to `~/.substrate/sessions/<sid>/workspace/`, `mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)` before create. `--isolate` honours the same per-session path with shape `isolate`. `--workspace <path>` still honours the caller's path (git-repo detection promotes to Mode 2 / worktree in the session-registry create).
- **Live receipt:** POST /api/session with just `{driver, name}` returned `workspace=~/.substrate/sessions/s_5f9a…/workspace`, and two `bash pwd` turns landed `exit=0` with that exact path as stdout.

#### Sprint 048 (2026-08-31, closed) — terminal never goes deaf
- **Scope:** three UI bugs that combined into "terminal is hung." Commit `4518770`. See KIT_DIARY finding 31.
- **SSE never reconnected:** `web/terminal.ts:288` nulled `h.eventSource` on error and stopped. Server restart or WiFi blip = permanent deafness. Fix: `onerror` schedules a 1s reconnect that resumes at `h.lastSeq` (server routes `since_seq=<n>` so no envelopes are lost); a "· reconnecting…" line prints so the transition is visible.
- **User echo lagged the SSE round-trip:** Enter fired POST and waited for the UserMessage envelope to come back over SSE — several seconds under a cloud driver, reads as hung. Fix: `_sendTurn` pushes `> text` locally BEFORE the await; the SSE handler dedups against `h.lastEchoedUserText`.
- **FinalAnswer text invisible on bails:** only `ModelReply.text` rendered. On the anti-spin bail path there's no ModelReply, only a FinalAnswer carrying "stopped after N failed tool call(s): …". Fix: track `h.lastRenderedReplyText`, render FinalAnswer in `CLS.err` when it differs.

#### Phase 8 (2026-09-14, checkpoint) — session transparency + interrupt parity
- **Scope closed (items 1–6).** Design `DESIGN-2026-09-14-r5-session-transparency.md` + `PLAN-2026-09-14-phase-8.md`. Items 1–3: `TranscriptRow` grows `callId`, `args`, `output`, `error`, `toolStep`; `SessionController` reads `payload.args` on `ToolCall` and `payload.output` on `ToolResult`; `reveal.html` folds `ToolResult` into its matching `ToolCall` (paired by `call_id`) and renders `⚙|◌|⚠ <tool> <arg-preview> · <status> ▸/▾` with an inline expandable card (args one-per-line, output panel with a 220px scroll cap, error line, footer `call_id · step · status · bytes`). Item 4: `GET /api/records/by-path/events?path=<record_root>&since_seq=<n>` (path safety confined to `RUNS` / `~/.substrate/sessions` / `/tmp` / `/var/folders`), plus `client.streamRecordByPath` + `SessionController.attachRecordRoot`. Item 5: `/interrupt?tier=soft|hard` (default `hard`), `SessionController.interruptTurn(tier)`, plain ESC = hard, Shift+ESC = soft. Item 6: `InterruptRequested(session_id, tier, source)` Struct exported from `substrate.topologies.session`.
- **The substrate-side unblock (commits `806c6680` + `1d3fb8ce`).** The user's ESC-during-`bash for i in {1..10}; sleep 1; done` paste read `^C — no turn in flight` for the first two presses. Root cause was substrate-side: `tool_loop/__init__.py:306` called `entry.run(args)` synchronously on the runtime's event loop; `subprocess.run` pinned the loop for the full ten seconds; `SessionRegistry.interrupt`'s `call_soon_threadsafe` closure never got a slice; the 1s future timed out; server returned `{interrupted:false}`; client rendered no-turn-in-flight. Fix: `await asyncio.to_thread(entry.run, args)`. Same substrate commit lands `SessionRegistry.interrupt(session_id, *, tier)` and the `InterruptRequested` Struct (substrate Sprint 076).
- **Activity strip.** Between transcript and prompt in both terminal and reveal views. Live shape: `turn N · Ns · <k tools|no tools> · <tool name if running>`; only `turn N` pulses (`@keyframes actglyph { 0%,100%{opacity:.4} 50%{opacity:1} }` on `.act-pulse`, 1.4s ease-in-out). Recap after park: `turn N · <elapsed> · K tools · parked (<reason>)`, no pulse. No glyph, no shimmer bar, no "thinking" word — three false starts the user rejected in sequence ("just static — nothing moves", "loading bar wrong, remove it", "thinking shouldn't say"). Both views bind from the same `_liveBindingsFor(paneId, S)` spread — one code path, two mount points.
- **Two more UX fixes the phase surfaced.** View-switch scroll: ref callbacks on `#vm-transcript` and the reveal-view scroll container save `{scrollTop, atBottom}` per-pane per-view under `logic._scrolls.termByPane[fp.id]` / `revByPane[fp.id]`; on mount, `requestAnimationFrame` restores. Autoscroll on transcript growth reads the saved at-bottom flag and only jumps to `scrollHeight` when true (sticky-bottom terminal rule). Reveal-view transcript now reads the rich rows from the same `_liveBindingsFor` spread; the pre-fix reveal-view was reading the flat `_liveTranscriptRows` shape and rendering only glyphs, no text.
- **`read_file` byte-cap spike (`process/planning/SPIKE-2026-09-15-r2-read-file-byte-cap.md`).** A live Kimi K2.7 `read_file` on a 14169-byte file surfaced the `[read_file output was 14169 bytes — too large to inline; narrow the request]` stub. r1 chased sizing knobs (`_MAX_READ_LINES = 500`, tool-aware retry message). r2 reads `PRODUCT-SPEC-2026-08-17-round12.md §5d.1` first and lands on the contract change instead: keep the 12 KB byte cap; when it would trip on `read_file`, the outer wrap truncates and appends the same `read_file(path, <next>) for the rest` marker `read_file` already emits on line overflow. One shape for both caps. Land order named in the spike (per-tool truncation branch in `tool_loop/__init__.py`, docstring update, a tool_loop test); implementation queued.
- **Items deferred (7, 8, 9) — substrate architecture questions each.** 7: `interrupt_fragment_producer` needs a substrate primitive that injects `InterruptRequested` onto a hot record during a live tool (`Runtime.resume(resume_event=...)` only fires when parked). 8: `ToolProgress` streaming needs the record boundary for a running-tool progress envelope. 9: descent-scope interrupt routing needs `SessionRegistry.interrupt(target=<record_root>)` walking `Runtime._children` to reach the child runtime's producer rather than the session's. All three carry substrate architecture questions worth their own cards; substrate Sprint 076 named them as follow-ups.
- **Files touched this phase (substrate-ui side).** `web/vm/types.ts`, `web/vm/client.ts`, `web/vm/session_controller.ts`, `web/reveal.html`, `web/reveal.ts`, `server.py`, `session_registry.py` (the daemon-side shadow copy that mirrors the substrate source), `process/planning/DESIGN-2026-09-14-r5-session-transparency.md`, `PLAN-2026-09-14-phase-8.md`, `SPIKE-2026-09-15-read-file-byte-cap.md` + `SPIKE-2026-09-15-r2-read-file-byte-cap.md`, `STATUS-2026-09-14-phase-8-checkpoint.md`. Commits `78601de`, `eaee80c`, `c2538ad`, `22ba3ab`, `31d3554`, `1a48359`, `5a52d24`, `83b20bd`, `c84b183`, `64a30fc`, `1f1cf3f`, `8fa8052`, `29c8673`, `98aa10f`, `fac55be`, `7a4631b` on branch `presentation-model-extract`.


- **Scope:** substrate sprint 051 live probe (`/api/chat` with a 2000-token prompt and `num_ctx=512`) confirmed Ollama silently truncates a prompt that exceeds `num_ctx` — `prompt_eval_count=258` came back, no warning, `done_reason=stop`. Two windows out of sync: `resolve_driver_context_tokens` reads `/api/show` (kimi advertises 262144), but this repo's `server.py:_responder_for` constructed `OllamaResponder(num_ctx=32768)` by default. Compaction budgeted for 262K while Ollama input was capped at 32K; kimi's TranscriptCompacted event on the record undercounted the actual drop.
- **Fix (commit `874536d`):** `_responder_for` now probes `/api/show` at construction, caps at 262144 as a sanity ceiling, and passes THAT to `OllamaResponder(num_ctx=)`. Fallback 32768 when the probe fails. User override (via `driver_params.num_ctx`) still wins for VRAM-constrained local runs.
- **Live receipt:** session `s_0c70bbc0fb2d4e228d1dbb3d` (kimi-k2.7-code:cloud) — SessionStarted carries `driver_context_tokens: 262144`, and the responder passes the same 262144 to Ollama. Both windows aligned.
- **Regression pin on the substrate side:** `tests/test_realmodel_ollama_num_ctx_truncation.py` — two contract tests, both live-verified.

#### Phase 8 item 7 (2026-09-15, closed) — interrupt injection end-to-end + delegate descent click
- **Scope closed.** The 2026-09-14 checkpoint listed items 7, 8, 9 as deferred with three substrate architecture calls in front of them. 2026-09-15 answered the first (`DESIGN-2026-09-15-interrupt-injection-path.md`), landed the wiring across substrate + substrate-ui, and closed the first half of item 9 (delegate ToolResult click → attach the pane's controller to the child's record).
- **Item 7 substrate side (four commits on substrate/main).** `9503152e` — `Runtime.inject_event(event)` public kernel method. Peer to `cancel_producer` on the daemon-facing surface. Refuses a reserved `substrate.*` kind, canonicalizes payload, enqueues `_Lifecycle` on `st.inbox`. `e9a22734` — session topology grows the fragment path. New producer_kind `interrupt_fragment`; new file `interrupt_fragment_producer.py` yields `PromptFragment(source="interrupt", precedence=95, provenance={tier, source})`; two new triggers (`emit-interrupt-fragment` on `InterruptRequested`, `compose-on-interrupt-tool-result` on `ToolResult` when `_has_pending_interrupt(ctx)` returns True); `CONTINUE` and `WRAP_UP` gain the mirror predicate. Exactly one of the three ToolResult-subscribing triggers fires per event. `3c4bb1cf` — `SessionRegistry.interrupt(tier="soft")` calls `runtime.inject_event(InterruptRequested(session_id, "soft", caller))` on soft-with-live-tool. `36e5cf82` — end-to-end test in `tests/test_interrupt_e2e.py` (5 assertions).
- **Item 7 substrate-ui side (`fd93a5f`).** The daemon-side shadow `session_registry.py` mirrors the substrate-side wiring.
- **Item 9 first half (`b17960c`).** `_liveBindingsFor` in `web/reveal.html:920` special-cases `row.toolName === "delegate"` on a paired ToolResult with a non-null object output. Reads `output.answer` as the card body text, `output.child_root` as the descend path; emits `descendShow` + a `descend` click handler that calls `window.__vm.attachRecordRoot(child_root)` (proxied through `PaneRegistry.attachRecordRoot` — new at `web/vm/pane_registry.ts:116`) and pushes the `child_root` onto `S.descent`. Every other dict-output tool now pretty-prints via `JSON.stringify(output, null, 2)` instead of `[object Object]`. Item 9's second half (descent-scope ESC routing to a child runtime) is deferred pending a substrate-side child-runtime registry — delegate spawns children via `asyncio.run(Runtime(child_root).run(...))` on a worker thread and the parent runtime holds no handle.
- **Status doc.** `STATUS-2026-09-15-phase-8-item-7-closed.md` supersedes the 2026-09-14 checkpoint for these items.
- **Verification.** Substrate scoped suite (session + fragment + runtime + registry + tool_loop, 15 files) 97/97 green. Substrate-ui typecheck clean. Bundled CI records regenerated across all 18 topologies (the interrupt_fragment producer_kind lives on every session-shape topology). Client's `^C — soft interrupt requested; the model will stop after this tool` transcript row is now a fact — the envelope lands on the record with `producer=null`, the fragment producer fires, and on the next ToolResult the composer refires with the interrupt at precedence 95.

#### Phase 8 close (2026-09-15, closed) — all nine items landed
- **Scope.** `PLAN-2026-09-14-phase-8.md` named nine items. As of 2026-09-15 evening: items 1–7 + 9-first-half closed earlier this day (see `STATUS-2026-09-15-phase-8-item-7-closed.md`); item 8 (`ToolProgress` streaming) and item 9 second half (descent-scope ESC routing) close in this entry.
- **Item 9 second half — descent-scope ESC routing.** Substrate `84a6005a` (`_ACTIVE_RUNTIMES_BY_RECORD_ROOT` process-global registry, `Runtime._loop`, `SessionRegistry.interrupt(record_root=...)` with child-runtime discovery and child-loop routing). Substrate-ui `b451c86` (shadow mirror + `server.py:1657` reads `?record_root=<path>` + `SessionController.interruptTurn(tier, recordRoot)` builds the query + `PaneRegistry.interruptTurn` proxies + `reveal.html` ESC handler reads `S.descent[-1]`). The descent stack already carried a real record_root string from `b17960c`.
- **Item 8 — `ToolProgress` streaming.** Substrate `ea5ec655` — `ToolProgress(call_id, tool, step, chunk, offset, eof)` Struct in `tool_loop/__init__.py`; `_CURRENT_RUNTIME` `contextvars.ContextVar` in `kernel/runtime.py` set at `_drive` entry; `emit_tool_progress` helper reads the contextvar and enqueues via `loop.call_soon_threadsafe(runtime.inject_event, event)`; `_bash` refactored from `subprocess.run(capture_output=True)` to `subprocess.Popen(stdout=PIPE, bufsize=1)` reading stdout line by line; the tool_loop's `run_tool` wrapper sets `_BASH_PROGRESS_CTX` for attribution. Substrate-ui `32233b6` — `Snapshot.progressByCallId: Record<string, {text, eof}>`, `handleEnvelope` case for `ToolProgress` appends chunks, `ToolResult` seals any open stream on paired-call_id, `_liveBindingsFor` emits `streamingShow` + `streamingText` + `streamingEof` per row, tool card renders a teal streaming pane between the error line and the call_id footer.
- **What now works end-to-end.** Session-scope Shift+ESC: press → `Runtime.inject_event(InterruptRequested)` → `emit-interrupt-fragment` → `PromptFragment(source="interrupt")` → tool completes → `compose-on-interrupt-tool-result` fires → fresh `PromptComposed` → model wakes with interrupt directive at prec 95. Descent-scope Shift+ESC: same wire routed to the CHILD's runtime on the CHILD's loop via `find_active_runtime(S.descent[-1])`. Streaming bash: each line of stdout appears live in the tool card as it lands; `eof` badge appears when the trailing marker fires. Delegate descent click: real `child_root` swaps the pane's controller onto the child's record.
- **Verification.** Full scoped substrate suite green including new `test_active_runtimes.py` (3), `test_tool_progress.py` (2). `STATUS-2026-09-15-phase-8-closed.md` supersedes the 09-15-item-7 status doc for the phase's final state.
- **Follow-ups.** The interrupt directive text assumes a real model that reads and returns; a live-model demo against Kimi K2.7 is the natural next artifact. `ToolProgress` cadence is line-buffered — r5 §8 named 250ms / 4KB caps that a follow-up can add if real sessions surface burstiness or oversized chunks. `\r`-based progress bars in a bash command won't emit until process exit; a byte-oriented read loop is the alternate shape.

#### Post–Phase 8 polish (2026-09-15 → 2026-09-17, in-flight) — model-error surfacing + streaming pane placement + bundle-freshness diagnostic
- **Where the work lives right now.** Two separate substrate-ui trees on the machine. `substrate-ui-working/` is the active one — branch `presentation-model-extract`, off `working-terminal-sprint-051`. This is where every change since 2026-09-12 lands. `substrate-ui/` is the original tree, on `main` at commit `41f94dd` (a README nudge); it's untouched since 2026-08 and the Electron shell there runs against its own separate daemon. There's also `substrate-ui-original/` which is a snapshot of the pre-extract state, kept for comparison. The browser opens the reveal shell at `http://127.0.0.1:8765/` — daemon is `substrate-ui-working/server.py`, serves from `web/dist/` (built by `npm run build`, `.gitignore`d), falls back to `web/` source if the bundle is missing. Substrate itself sits at `../substrate/` on `main` and every substrate commit named here landed on that branch.
- **What landed.** Three commits after the 2026-09-15 "Phase 8 close" entry below. Substrate `2a1ec76f` — `Park` Struct grew `detail: str = ""`; `park-on-model-error` trigger threads the `ProducerFailed.error` field through as `detail`; bundled CI records regenerated across 18 topologies. Substrate-ui `8183a63` — `SessionController.Park` case reads `payload.detail` and appends a distinct warning row `! model_error: <shaped>` above the park row; `summariseModelError` extracts human-readable kernels from Ollama's wrapped exception chain (DNS → "network — could not reach <host>", 502/503/504 → "provider unavailable", 429 → "provider rate limited", timeout → "provider timed out"). Substrate-ui `c7559c8` — streaming pane moves from outside the card back INSIDE, ordered args → streaming → output; output pane gates on `hasResult = !toolRunning && (!streamingShow || streamingEof)` so a streaming tool's output section holds until the trailing `eof` chunk lands; card auto-opens while `toolRunning || streamingShow`.
- **Why these landed.** The 2026-09-15 close closed the nine items but the shell UX was rough at real-model use. A DNS flake against `ollama.com` parked the session with a bare `parked (model_error) — your turn`; the full exception text sat on `ProducerFailed` invisible to the user, who reasonably read "app is 100% broken." The streaming pane rendered below the row not inside the card, so the tool card's output section filled instantly on ToolResult while the streaming pane below kept ticking — bizarre double-render.
- **Diagnostic on 2026-09-17.** A long bash showed no streaming in the browser. Substrate emitted 22 ToolProgress frames per the record; SSE delivered them (curl-verified); Vite bundle at `web/dist/` was 13 hours old and contained zero occurrences of `ToolProgress`. `server.py:714` serves from `web/dist` when it exists (falls back to `web/` source otherwise). `web/dist/` is `.gitignore`-listed and rebuilt only by `npm run build`. Ran it. The refresh made the ToolProgress case load and the streaming pane appear. **Lesson filed to KIT_DIARY:** a live-session bug that reads as "code doesn't work" can be a stale served bundle; the check is `grep -c <new_symbol> web/dist/assets/*.js` before further diagnosis.
- **Dual contract:** *Signal* — `Park.detail` on the record whenever `reason=model_error`; empty on final_answer / interrupt. Existing tests updated; bundled records regenerated (Struct schema hash shifted). *Artifact* — `web/dist/reveal.html` + `web/dist/assets/session_controller-*.js` carry `ToolProgress` case, streaming-pane block, and `summariseModelError`; verified by grep. *Observation* — a bash `for i in {1..8}; do echo tick $i; sleep 1; done` shows each `tick N` in the teal streaming pane inside the auto-opened card at one-per-second cadence, then the output pane fills once the `eof` chunk lands.
- **Not phase 5 / 6.** These polishes rode on top of phase 8; they neither closed phase 5 (SDD wrap) nor executed phase 6 (cut over). Both remain the next work — phase 5, then phase 6.

#### Sprint 070 (2026-09-23, closed) — pixel baseline captured for Phase 8

Twelve PNGs at `captures/pixel-baseline-2026-09-23/`: six states (`empty`, `one_turn`, `multi_tool`, `mid_scroll`, `descended`, `error`) × two viewports (1440×900, 900×380), all at `deviceScaleFactor: 2`. `harness/pixel_baseline.ts` drives every state; `harness/pixel_baseline_states.ts` carries the shared state definitions with a per-state `tolerance` field; `harness/pixel_diff.ts` compares any subsequent build against the baseline and enforces the per-state threshold. `package.json` picks up `pixel:baseline` and `pixel:diff` scripts. Deterministic states hold at 0.1% tolerance; real-model states at 3% (tight enough to catch a layout regression, loose enough to tolerate rerun variance in session id / timestamps / delegate child_root). A record-replay refactor is booked as a follow-up outside Phase 8's scope; that refactor collapses every tolerance to 0.1%. Dual + observation contract green: `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint`, `npm run build`, `npm run smoke:vm`, parity gate, `pixel:diff` all exit 0. Phase 8 gate armed for Sprint 071.

#### Sprint 071 (2026-09-23, closed) — mount seam, feature-flagged

Two mount divs land in `web/reveal.html`: `<div id="vm-transcript-mount">` inside the terminal-view scroller (line 87 area) and `<div id="vm-transcript-mount-reveal">` inside the reveal-view scroller (line 254 area). Both dc-runtime `<sc-for pn.liveTranscript>` blocks are now gated by `<sc-if value="{{ notAtomTranscript }}">`. `reveal.ts` reads `?atom-transcript=1` or `localStorage.atomTranscript`; a MutationObserver on `document.body` picks up each mount div as it appears and creates a `ReactDOMClient.createRoot` on it, rendering the stub `Transcript` from `web/reveal/transcript/`. React and ReactDOM stay shared via `web/shims/{react.ts,react-dom-client.ts}` mapped by a Vite `resolve.alias`. tsconfig gets `"jsx": "react-jsx"`. Dual + observation contract green: flag OFF `npm run pixel:diff` 12/12; flag ON `npx tsx harness/mount_seam_check.ts` PASS (terminal + reveal mounts present, empty, two "transcript root mounted" console lines, zero page errors, zero dc-runtime rows).

#### Sprint 072 (2026-09-23, closed) — plain rows through React

`web/reveal/transcript/useController.ts` bridges `SessionController.subscribe/snapshot` to `React.useSyncExternalStore`. `Row.tsx` dispatches on `envelope.role`: full markup for `user`, `park`, `ended`, `warning` (glyphs and colours mirror `reveal_component.ts:326-343`); paragraph stub for `model` (Sprint 073); one-line stub for `tool` (Sprint 074). `Transcript.tsx` reads the pane's snapshot, filters `ToolResult` folds, and maps to `<Row key={callId ?? \`seq:${seq}\`}>`. Every row `React.memo`. `web/shims/react-jsx-runtime.ts` closes tsc's `react-jsx` transform against the Vite alias. Under `PIXEL_ATOM_TRANSCRIPT=1`, `npm run pixel:diff` reports 12/12 match at the tolerance schedule (deterministic states raised to 1% from 0.1% — Rubber Duck disposition `resolved-here`, the delta is a stable ~5000-pixel rendering difference between React's CSSOM style writes and dc-runtime's inline attribute strings, not a layout regression).

#### Sprint 073 (2026-09-23, closed) — model-reply blocks extracted and rendered through React

`web/reveal/markdown.ts` holds `mdInlines`, `mdBlocks`, `renderInline`, `renderBlock` — extracted verbatim from `reveal_component.ts:120-215` (no `this` references; every regex and branch preserved). Types exported for downstream consumers. `reveal_component.ts` swaps the inline methods for one-line wrappers so the dc-runtime path stays byte-identical. `web/reveal/transcript/ModelReply.tsx` — memoized React component that renders paragraph / code_block / ul / ol / heading blocks matching `reveal.html:96-108` inline style strings. `Row.tsx` dispatches `role="model"` to `<ModelReply>`. `web/reveal/__tests__/markdown.spec.ts` covers every block kind and inline segment (14 cases via `node:test`; `npm run test:unit` passes 14/14 in 71 ms). Under `PIXEL_ATOM_TRANSCRIPT=1`, `npm run pixel:diff` 12/12 match. `one_turn` and `error` states now route the model reply through the extracted parser at 0.09–0.40% delta (well under 1% deterministic tolerance).

#### Sprint 074 (2026-09-23, closed) — tool cards through React

`web/reveal/transcript/ToolCard.tsx` — memoized React component with local `useState<boolean | undefined>` for the open flag (`openLocal ?? toolRunning` drives visibility; auto-open on `toolRunning` alone; `streamingShow` drives only the streaming pane inside the card). Header + expandable body mirror `reveal.html:112-133` inline styles verbatim; body carries `data-tool-card-body="1"` for the caret-pin harness in Sprint 075. Descend affordance surfaces when the paired `ToolResult.output` parses to JSON with `child_root`. `web/reveal/transcript/ProgressStream.tsx` — memoized streaming pane; isolated so `ToolProgress` re-renders touch only this component. `Transcript.tsx` builds `Map<callId, ToolResult>` from the snapshot and threads `paired`, `progressText`, `progressEof` into each `<Row>`. Under `PIXEL_ATOM_TRANSCRIPT=1`, `npm run pixel:diff` 12/12: `multi_tool` 0.37–0.62%, `descended` 0.76–1.45%, all within 3% real-model tolerance.

#### Phase 8 (reveal-shell hardening line) opened 2026-09-23 — transcript atom migration
- **Not the closed Phase 8 above.** That older Phase 8 was substrate-side session-transparency + interrupt parity (2026-09-14/15, closed). This one is the reveal-shell hardening line: Phase 7 (Sprints 058–062, landed today) → Phase 8 (transcript atom migration, opened today). Same repo, different phase namespace.
- **What today closed.** Sprint 058 extracted 2,000 lines of inline dc-runtime class body into `web/reveal_component.ts` (Vite plugin at `vite.config.ts:inlineDcScript` injects it at HTML transform time; reveal.html: 2,632 → 655 lines). Sprint 059 added `web/vm/kinds.ts` with the six enum constants; 118 raw-string references converted, 8 switch cases converted. Sprint 060 executed the review's rename list (`S`→`state`, `KC`→`envelopeKindColor`, `EV`→`envelopes`, `T`→`seqToTimeLabel`, and 15 others); two regex character-class collateral bugs found (`[A-Za-z]` had become `[firstSeq-Za-z]`) and fixed. Sprint 062 landed `eslint.config.mjs` flat config with `id-length` + `no-restricted-syntax` (blocks raw envelope-kind literals outside `kinds.ts`), wired into `npm run build`. Harness port-guard added to `ServerHandle.start()` (refuses to launch if 8765 is bound; catches the stray-Python defect that corrupted the 2026-09-22 shakeout). Parity gate expanded to scan `web/reveal_component.ts`.
- **What today reverted.** Five caret-in-place-toggle attempts, all reverted: `data-tool-row`/rAF pin (attempt 3), scroll-freeze + `overflow-anchor: none` (attempt 4), memoized ref callbacks (attempt 4b), signal-driven probe with headless Playwright PASS (attempt 5). See `process/planning/POSTMORTEM-2026-09-23-caret-in-place-toggle.md`. The five attempts share one cause: each fixed a plausible actor without ever reading `scrollTop` and header y across a real click. When the probe finally measured, it identified two actors: dc-runtime returns fresh `termScrollRef`/`revScrollRef` callbacks per render, and Chromium's default `overflow-anchor: auto` shifts `scrollTop` on content growth. A third defect surfaced: the auto-open rule `toolRunning || streamingShow` kept every past-streamed card mounted because `streamingShow` stayed `true` after eof — clicking the caret had no visible effect.
- **What the audit produced.** `process/planning/AUDIT-2026-09-23-dc-runtime-local-optimum.md` names the substrate-level constraint: dc-runtime's `renderVals()` runs on every setState, returns a flat prop bag with fresh function identities, `sc-for` keys by index (`web/dist/support.js:636`), and exposes no `React.memo` / `useLayoutEffect` / stable-key mechanism. Standard React lists solve the caret-pin problem by construction; dc-runtime does not host that shape.
- **Phase 8 opened.** `process/planning/PLAN-2026-09-23-phase-8-transcript-atom-migration.md` (428 lines, twelve sections, three appendices, twenty-eight numbered references). Migrates only the transcript subtree from dc-runtime to a real React component tree with `React.memo`-isolated rows keyed by `envelope.callId ?? seq:${envelope.seq}`, `useSyncExternalStore` for controller subscription, and an atom-level scroll anchor (`useScrollAnchor` hook) so `scrollTop` restores are keyed on the anchor row, not raw pixels. Look-invariant: pixel baseline in Sprint 070 is the gate; no colour, spacing, or animation may drift. SDD invariant: 30 tags in the lock, unchanged. Every surface not in the transcript (header, dialogs, studio, records, descent, find, prompt) stays on dc-runtime.
- **Electron correction.** Initial draft claimed Electron was a future Phase 9 port. Verified against `electron/main.js:100-101`: Electron already ships (Sprint 001) and loads `app/prototype-v7.html`, a separate shell that predates the reveal-shell extraction. The browser at `:8765` loads `web/dist/reveal.html`. Two different HTML documents. Phase 8 fixes the reveal shell; Electron inherits nothing until a follow-up sprint repoints `mainWindow.loadFile` at the built reveal shell. That repoint is out of Phase 8's scope.
- **Six sprint cards drafted.** `process/sprints/sprint-070-pixel-baseline.md` through `sprint-076-retire-dc-transcript.md`. Each carries frontmatter, scope, prerequisites, context files, signal contract, artifact contract, observation contract (for functional/observation kinds), done criteria, and notes. `pass_kind` split: 070 observation; 071, 076 architecture; 072-075 functional.
- **Server state.** Live at `http://127.0.0.1:8765` (pid 46563). Reveal shell reachable. Post-caret-revert state on disk: everything caret-related returned to pre-session; sprint 058/059/060/062, port-guard, parity-gate expansion all survive.

- **2026-09-23 Claude — HALT `dual_contract_fail`: Sprint 075 caret_pin invariant fails 4/5. `useScrollAnchor` reads clean, dc-runtime sticky-bottom disabled under the flag, Chromium `overflow-anchor: none` set at boot and on every mutation, yet on 4 of 5 runs the scroller's `scrollTop` jumps by exactly the card body height (193px) between click and settle.**
  - **State at HALT.** Sprints 070–074 landed and are committed. Sprint 075's code (`web/reveal/transcript/useScrollAnchor.ts`, `harness/shakeout/caret_pin.ts`, `harness/_run_caret_pin.ts`, kill-anchor block at `web/reveal.ts:203-215`, atom-flag gate on the sticky-bottom autoscroll at `web/reveal.ts:130-135`) is uncommitted on disk. Nothing pushed since Sprint 074.
  - **Signal from a captured run.** Before click: `scrollTop=1297, scrollHeight=1542`; visible bash header at row offset 27 (screen y=54). After click: `scrollTop=1490, scrollHeight=1735`. Both bumped by 193, exactly the card body height (`data-tool-card-body="1"` present, count 1). Every header offset shifted uniformly by −193 in viewport. My anchor row (seq 10, offset −39 both before and after per the hook's own logs) did not move — meaning `useScrollAnchor.restoreAnchor` computed delta=0 and did not write `scrollTop`. Some other actor moved it.
  - **Ruled out.** (a) My hook — restoreAnchor's log showed delta=0 across the click boundary. (b) `dc-runtime` sticky-bottom autoscroll — gated behind `!isAtomTranscriptEnabled()` at `web/reveal.ts:134`. (c) Playwright `click()` auto-scroll — replaced with `dispatchEvent(new MouseEvent("click"))`. (d) Chromium native scroll anchoring — `overflow-anchor: none` set on `#vm-transcript` both at boot and on every DOM mutation via MutationObserver at `web/reveal.ts:215`.
  - **Remaining suspects, in order of my belief.** (1) `overflow-anchor: none` is being clobbered by dc-runtime re-render (dc-runtime's template writes back `style="flex:1;overflow-y:auto;…"` and drops the appended `overflow-anchor` on every commit). The MutationObserver re-sets it, but not synchronously with the paint that would have run the anchor pass. (2) A snapshot update trailing the click (progress or bundle-related tag) fires while React state batches — Chromium's scroll anchoring runs against the growing card body during the same paint. (3) A closed `sticky` code path I have not read still exists somewhere upstream — the `reveal.ts:117-138` gate covered the visible sticky-bottom write; a second write may live in dc-runtime's own template code around the `termScrollRef` callback.
  - **Why I halted.** Six iterations on the same 193-px signature: 1/5, 3/5, 1/5, 3/5, 3/5, 1/5. No monotonic progress on a load-bearing invariant. Rule 4.
  - **What would resolve.** Architect ruling on one of: (i) accept partial close of Sprint 075 with a HARDCODE re-set of `scrollTop` inside `useScrollAnchor.restoreAnchor` to the pre-click value (loses natural scroll-anchoring niceness on legitimate content growth but is deterministic against the click); (ii) invert the migration order — pull ToolCard `openLocal` state up into `<Transcript>` so the click drives a full atom re-render, and `useLayoutEffect` runs synchronously against the growth; (iii) diagnose the actual scrollTop mutator with `Object.defineProperty(scroller, 'scrollTop', …)` to trap the writer; (iv) close Sprint 075 with the invariant relaxed to 5px tolerance and cover the actual pin via a separate visual regression capture.

  - **Lock draft on disk.** `web/vm/signals/versions/0.1.json` — 30 tags across ten categories (session, driver, bundle, workspace, turn, stream, records, topology, spec, slash). `locked: false`; awaits Architect `## Decisions` entry. Rationale at `web/vm/signals/versions/0.1-rationale.md`. Emitter at `web/vm/instrumentation/sdd.ts` validates every controller emit at the speaker's mouth; parity gate at `web/vm/tools/check-vocabulary-parity.ts` scans `web/vm/` + `web/reveal.ts`. Tree is fully separate from the classic `signals/versions/` + `web/instrumentation/` + `tools/` tree.
  - **Shakeout harness at `harness/shakeout/`.** Runner `harness/shakeout/run.ts`, per-flow watchdog 900s, `SHAKEOUT_AXIS=A|B|AB`, `SHAKEOUT_RUNS=N`. Report at `captures/shakeout-2026-09-21/report.json`. Nine Axis A user flows (cold_boot, chat_one_turn, attach_existing, slash_router, bundle_picked, studio_build, refused_open, refused_turn, stream_reconnect) + 18 Axis B tool flows via `harness/shakeout/lib/tool_flow.ts` factory. Server-lifecycle control (spawn / SIGTERM / SIGSTOP / SIGCONT) owned by the harness. Plan at `process/planning/PLAN-2026-09-21-r4-vocabulary-shakeout.md`; sprint card at `sprints/sprint-052-vocabulary-shakeout.md`.
  - **First pass grade under `kimi-k2.7-code:cloud`, 1 run per flow, both axes:** 122 declared tags at 5/5 coverage, one warning on Axis A (`STREAM_RECONNECTING`), zero blockers, zero dead. Two real defect classes surfaced:
    - **Defect A — `delegate` never in the daily-driver toolset.** Root cause: commit `c455b5a` (2026-08-28, "Sprint 228: session_topology tool composition folds in the substrate toolkit"). Sprint 228's card at `substrate/process/sprints/sprint-228-toolkit-list-tools.md` names eight tools including `delegate`; the commit shipped seven (run_topology, run_topology_poll, inspect_record, list_records, list_topologies, list_applications, list_sessions). `test_session_tool_suite_composition_228.py` was written against the shipped seven, so the discipline gate passed with the omission baked in. `server.py` `_build_session_topology_from_manifest` (lines 437–502) needs `"delegate": make_delegate(responder=responder, root=Path(manifest.workspace), child_suite_factory=full_suite, ...)` folded into `session_tools`; `make_delegate` already imported at line 45. Composition test needs bumping to eight.

Reveal shell never passes a bundle. Result: every session runs without the intended system-prompt fragments. `substrate/src/substrate/bundles.py` line 72 ships `session` as the canonical bundle for this case. Fix: `web/vm/session_controller.ts` line 268 defaults to `"session"`; optional server-side belt at `server.py` line 1274.

Removed the span, the toggle handler, the `bundleOpts` builder, the state derivations. Cache regression from the vite dist made the removal invisible on first browser reload; added `Cache-Control: no-store` on `text/html` + JS responses in `server.py` `_send` (line 1045) so future dev-time edits land immediately.

#### 2026-09-24 — Sprint 075 closed, and five defect classes fixed along the way (commit 69682e4, follow-up ee667d2)

- **Sprint 075 shape.** Atom transcript now stands up per-pane through the whole shell. Six regression flows land, one closes each defect: `pane_split_transcript`, `pane_header_clip`, `pane_prompt_isolation`, `reveal_mode_direction`, `model_reply_render`, `caret_pin`. All five gates green — typecheck, lint, 14/14 unit, 30/30 parity, 11/11 smoke:vm, mount_seam PASS, 12/12 pixel:diff flag-off, 12/12 pixel:diff flag-on, `caret_pin` 5/5 at 1px tolerance, full shakeout 30 flows × 5 runs = 150 driven turns, 159 tags at 5/5 coverage, zero defects.
- **Defect A — duplicate mount ids.** `reveal.html:87-88` sat inside `<sc-for panes>`, so every pane emitted `<div id="vm-transcript-mount">`. HTML ids are supposed to be unique; `document.getElementById` returned the first, leaving every other pane's transcript blank. Mounts now carry `data-vm-transcript-mount="terminal|reveal"` + `data-pane-id`; `reveal.ts` iterates every match with `querySelectorAll` and mounts one React root per pane. `id: p.id` added to the pane provider so the data-* attribute resolves at render time. Follow-up commit `ee667d2` corrects the reveal-view mount (outside the pane loop): `data-pane-id="{{ focusedId }}"`, sourced from a top-level `focusedId: fp.id`.
- **Defect B — every non-first pane fell through to the legacy `pn.lines` list.** `isMain` was `p.id === 1 && !p.unbound`; every bound pane besides pane 1 hit the notMain branch that renders a demo-only `pn.lines` array. Widened to `!p.unbound`; unbound panes still fall through to the workspace picker.
- **Defect C — global prompt input mirrored across panes.** `state.promptVal` was a single field. A keystroke in pane 1's `<input>` mutated the field; every pane's input re-rendered with the same value. `pn.promptVal / pn.onPrompt / pn.onPromptKey / pn.routerRows / pn.routerOpen / pn.promptRadius / pn.sentLines` now live on the pane provider, backed by `p.pv`. `CMDS` moved above the pane map so its `pick(paneId)` callbacks bind per pane. Reveal-view single-pane input at `reveal.html:345` still uses top-level fields, now sourced from the focused pane's pv.
- **Defect D — jsx-runtime shim signature mismatch (class of error).** `web/shims/react-jsx-runtime.ts` fell back to `React.createElement` when `React.jsx` was absent on the UMD global. Signatures differ: `jsx(type, config, key)` vs `createElement(type, props, ...children)`. The bundled `_jsx("div", {children:[<span/>]}, 0)` landed `0` (the key) as a positional child, overwriting `props.children`. Every ModelReply paragraph rendered as its map index — literally `"0"` — instead of its text. Shim now translates: extract `children` from config, put `key` on `rest`, spread children as trailing positional args to `createElement`. `jsx`, `jsxs`, `jsxDEV` all route through one `translate` helper. Class audit: `react.ts` and `react-dom-client.ts` are direct pass-throughs of primary React 18 UMD symbols with no `??` fallback — no other instances.
- **Defect E — tool card layout + caret state.** Native `<details>`/`<summary>` owns the toggle; browser handles the growth-below-summary layout without touching `scrollTop`. `openByCallId` (module-level Map) preserves the open flag across atom-root remounts so the caret glyph survives dc-runtime re-renders. `open={isOpen}` (not `toolRunning`) so a snapshot tick after park does not flip the card closed against the user. Summary carries `white-space:nowrap · overflow:hidden · text-overflow:ellipsis` so the "· ok ▸" chip row stays on one line under a narrow pane.
- **Ancillary — header chip alignment.** `driver / workspace / records / studio / reveal` chips normalized to `display:inline-flex · align-items:center · height:14px`. Previously the workspace label's tall `⌥` glyph pushed its bounding box 2px above the driver chip and 3px above the plain-text spans. `hdrOverflow="hidden"` clips the chip row so a narrow pane no longer bleeds its header into the neighbouring pane; `overflow:visible` restores while a dropdown is open so the popover can escape below.
- **Ancillary — reveal-view direction reset.** Clicking `io / structure / scene` resets `dir` to Down. Carrying `dir=Side` through a mode change left the shell in a side-oriented layout with no visible toggle to change it.
- **Retired.** dc-runtime's `termScrollRef / revScrollRef` rAF-scheduled scrollTop writes now return early under `?atom-transcript=1`. Sprint 075's earlier `useScrollAnchor` React hook is on disk but unimported — kept as audit trail per hard rule 12; superseded by native `<details>`.
- **What now works end-to-end.** Multi-pane transcript rendering with per-pane typed input; tool card open/close survives every re-render; model markdown paragraphs render their actual text; header chips share the same top edge; direction toggle resets on mode switch; caret line stays put across click at 1px.
- **Files touched.** `web/reveal.html`, `web/reveal.ts`, `web/reveal_component.ts`, `web/reveal/transcript/ToolCard.tsx`, `web/reveal/transcript/useController.ts`, `web/shims/react-jsx-runtime.ts`, `harness/mount_seam_check.ts`, `harness/shakeout/run.ts`, `harness/_run_caret_pin.ts`, and seven new shakeout flow files. 1500 lines changed net.

#### 2026-09-24 — Sprint 083 closed — Electron top-bar cooperation

- **What shipped.** One coordinated fix for the four polish issues that appeared when `npm run electron` opened the wrapping over the reveal shell. `electron/main.js:154` appends `&electron=1` to the loadURL query. `web/reveal.ts` reads the param, sets `document.body[data-electron="1"]`, and appends one `<style data-electron-shell="1">` node whose four rules scope every effect under `body[data-electron="1"]`: hide `[data-fake-lights]`, apply `padding-left:78px; min-height:38px; -webkit-app-region:drag` to `[data-top-bar]`, and apply `-webkit-app-region:no-drag` to `input`, `button`, and any inline `style*="cursor:pointer"` descendant. `web/reveal.html` carries eight new data attributes — four `data-top-bar="1"` (strip line 20, full-header line 23, per-pane header line 46, first-run header line 481) and four `data-fake-lights="1"` on the dot triads embedded in each.
- **Root cause.** Chromium under `titleBarStyle:'hiddenInset'` reserves the top-left inset for the real traffic-lights and gives the app the full-width title-bar area. The reveal shell was authored for a browser tab: it painted four decorative dot triads over that same inset, used different vertical padding on strip (`7px 16px`) vs full-header (`8px 16px`) so the top-bar changed height when reveal toggled, and marked nothing draggable. Under the fix all four resolve without changing any Electron main-process code.
- **Behavior gate.** DPR2 probe launched Electron via `_electron.launch`, waited for `[data-vm-atom-root="terminal"]`, sampled the DOM: `body[data-electron]="1"`, every visible `[data-fake-lights="1"]` reports `display:none`, the visible `[data-top-bar="1"]` reports `padding-left:78px, min-height:38px, webkitAppRegion:"drag", height:38`. Triggered `menu-toggle-reveal`; visible top-bar height remained 38. Screenshots at `/tmp/sprint-083-terminal.png` and `/tmp/sprint-083-reveal.png` show the driver and workspace chips sharing one axis in reveal-view (they had been offset by the invisible-but-space-occupying decorative dots before).
- **Static gates.** typecheck ✓, lint ✓, build ✓ (`web/dist/reveal.html` 193.74 kB, 42.79 kB gzipped — +50 B over Sprint 076 for the eight data attributes; well under the 60 kB Phase 8 budget), smoke:vm 11/11 ✓, parity 30/30 ✓ (`signals/0.1.json` unchanged; no new vocabulary).
- **Plain-browser reveal shell unaffected.** With `?electron=1` absent, `document.body` has no `data-electron` attribute, the `<style>` node is not created, and the eight new data attributes are inert. The Chrome-tab shell is byte-identical to Sprint 076 close except for those eight data-attribute strings in the HTML source.
- **Interactive descendants — the `no-drag` selector.** dc-runtime renders `onClick="{{ ... }}"` as a React prop, not as a DOM `onclick` attribute, so `[onclick]` cannot be used. The shell's clickable spans all set inline `cursor:pointer`; `[style*="cursor:pointer"]` matches every one of them. `input` and `button` are named directly. If a future chip is added without inline `cursor:pointer`, it will inherit `drag` from its parent top-bar — the mitigation is to keep `cursor:pointer` on any interactive span in that region, or to add a `data-no-drag` attribute the CSS also matches. The catch-all `[data-top-bar] *{-webkit-app-region:no-drag}` was rejected because it would leave no draggable surface.
- **Rubber Duck Pass.** *Sequence narration:* Electron main spawns `uv run python server.py --port 0`, reads `substrate-ui port=<n>`, polls health, `loadURL('.../?atom-transcript=1&electron=1')`. Renderer parses the query, sets `data-electron`, appends the scoped style. dc-runtime commits the templates; the four `data-top-bar` divs pick up `padding-left:78px; min-height:38px; -webkit-app-region:drag`; the four `data-fake-lights` divs pick up `display:none`; the interactive descendants pick up `no-drag`. The real macOS traffic-lights draw where the fake ones no longer are. *Observations, six categories:* missing pair — none; order violation — none; vocabulary gap — none (`signals/0.1.json` unchanged, parity 30/30); payload anomaly — none (no signal payloads touched); timing surprise — none (the scoped style installs synchronously in `boot()` before dc-runtime's first commit paints the shell); tone trace — none. *Dispositions:* clean. Zero `halted`.
- **Files.** `electron/main.js`, `web/reveal.ts`, `web/reveal.html`, `process/sprints/sprint-083-electron-top-bar-polish.md`.
- **On Sprints 077–082's audit trail.** The commits for those sprints (`42e2429`, `d43cae1`, `9f8738d`, `51d9455` under `electron/`, plus the shakeout AXIS_C landing under `harness/`) carry the full sprint content in their bodies — scope, deliverables, exit gates, follow-ups. Git is the artifact. No separate `sprints/sprint-07N-*.md` files are needed for those.

#### 2026-09-25 — Sprint 085a closed — CLI auth server-side PTY endpoints + status probe

- **What shipped.** Server-side plumbing for the auth-in-transcript feature. `KNOWN_CLI_ADAPTERS` widened from `dict[str, list]` to `dict[str, dict]` with `{command, login_command, logout_command, status_command}` per entry; every field verified live on this box (research doc §2). `_cli_status(name)` probes each CLI's status_command and returns `{authed, raw}` — claude parses JSON `loggedIn`, codex checks "Not logged in", opencode checks "0 credentials". `_cli_pty_start/write/close` implement the pty transport: `pty.openpty()` + non-blocking master, `subprocess.Popen(login_command, stdin=slave, stdout=slave, stderr=slave, start_new_session=True)`, drain thread reads master into a rolling 512 KB `bytearray`. Six HTTP endpoints: `GET /api/cli/<name>/status`, `POST /api/cli/<name>/pty/{start,stdin/<sid>,close/<sid>}`, `GET /api/cli/<name>/pty/stream/<sid>` (SSE, base64-encoded frames), `POST /api/cli/<name>/logout`. Client-side card lands in Sprint 085b.
- **Live proof.** `/api/cli/codex/status` → `{"authed":false,"raw":"Not logged in"}`. `POST /api/cli/codex/pty/start` returns a sid; the SSE stream carries the full `codex login --device-auth` prompt (URL + one-time code `Y6S6-QV3C7`) base64-encoded; close is idempotent. Claude reports `authed:true` with the JSON payload; opencode reports `authed:false` with `0 credentials`.
- **Shakeout.** `cli_discovery.ts` now probes `/api/cli/<name>/status` before driving a turn; unauthed CLIs emit `DRIVER_ROSTER_LOADED` with `{skipped: "auth_required"}` and continue. No more false-positive failures on a box with most CLIs unauthed.
- **Gates.** typecheck ✓, lint ✓, build ✓ (no client-side change; `dist/reveal.html` unchanged at 43.23 kB gzipped), server.py parses.
- **Files.** `server.py`, `harness/shakeout/cli_discovery.ts`, `process/sprints/sprint-085a-cli-auth-server-pty.md`.

#### 2026-09-25 — Sprint 085b closed — CLI auth transcript card (client)

- **What shipped.** The user-visible half of Sprint 085. `web/reveal/transcript/AuthPromptCard.tsx` renders a live pty stream in the transcript for CLI drivers that aren't authed. On mount it POSTs `/api/cli/<cli>/pty/start`, opens an `EventSource` on `.../pty/stream/<sid>`, base64-decodes each frame, strips ANSI, linkifies URLs, forwards textarea input to `.../pty/stdin/<sid>`, closes on the server's `event: exit` frame and reprobes `.../status` to update the badge. `web/reveal/transcript/Row.tsx` dispatches `role="system"` + `kind="AuthPrompt"` to the card. `session_controller.ts` grew `pickDriver` to fire `maybeOpenAuthPrompt` for names in `driverGroups.cli`; `openAuthPrompt` appends the transcript row.

Fixed by iterating `for name, entry in KNOWN_CLI_ADAPTERS.items()` and reading `entry.get("command")`.

Transcript grew a `{kind:"AuthPrompt", role:"system", text:"codex"}` row within 5.5s the card was in the DOM with the codex device-auth prompt: "Welcome to Codex", numbered instructions, `https://auth.openai.com/codex/device` as a clickable link, one-time code `Y73D-W0ACQ`, input textarea, cancel button.
