#### 2026-09-25 — Phase 9 CLI-auth arc closes — Sprint 085c + follow-ups

- **What shipped.** `web/reveal/transcript/ansi.ts` — new, ~200 lines. `AnsiTerminal` class: virtual grid + cursor position + SGR state; handles SGR (colors, bold, dim, reverse, 256-color 38/48;5;n), cursor moves (CUU/CUD/CUF/CUB), absolute position (CUP/HVP), save/restore (DECSC/DECRC), erase-in-line (EL), erase-in-display (ED), plus `\r \n \b \t`. `pending` buffer carries a partial CSI across SSE chunks. `keyToBytes(event)` translates a browser `KeyboardEvent` to the byte sequence a pty expects (arrows → `\x1b[A/B/C/D`, Enter → `\r`, Backspace → `\x7f`, Ctrl-`<letter>` → `\x01..\x1a`).
- **`web/reveal/transcript/AuthPromptCard.tsx` rewritten.** `<pre>`+`<textarea>` gone. Focusable `<div tabIndex={0} onKeyDown>` routes keystrokes through `keyToBytes` and POSTs the bytes to `/api/cli/<cli>/pty/stdin/<sid>`. Each SSE frame is base64→`Uint8Array`→`TextDecoder("utf-8",{stream:true})` so multibyte UTF-8 (box glyphs like `┌│└`) does not split into replacement chars across frames. Terminal render: `snapshot().rows` mapped to `<div>`s with color runs from SGR state; plain-text runs URL-linkified so the codex device-auth URL stays clickable. Card unmount POSTs `pty/close` so the server-side pty terminates when the row is swept.
- **Sweep on driver switch.** `pickDriver` filters `kind === "AuthPrompt"` rows out of the transcript before the auth card call. `AuthPromptCard`'s `useEffect` cleanup fires (POSTs `pty/close` for the old CLI), then `maybeOpenAuthPrompt` appends a fresh row for the new CLI. No stacking cards; server-side ptys terminate cleanly.
- **Catalog cleanup.** Removed from `KNOWN_CLI_ADAPTERS`: `gemini` (deprecated upstream), `aider` (no login command — env-per-invocation; returns with the future API-key dropdown section), `opencode` (Peter's ruling: not our business for now). Remaining: `claude`, `codex`, `cursor-agent`.
- **Two other polish landings this session.**
  - Ollama `think:true` on non-thinking models 400-fix (commit `6e190e7`). `_daemon_driver_resolver` now probes `/api/show` for the model's `capabilities` list and defaults `think` based on whether `"thinking"` is present. `huihui_ai/qwen2.5-coder-abliterate:7b` → `think:false`; `kimi-k2.7-code:cloud` → `think:true`. Explicit `driver_params["think"]` still wins.
  - Electron external links (commit `e00885d`). `webContents.setWindowOpenHandler` + `will-navigate` route any URL whose origin differs from `127.0.0.1:<port>` through `shell.openExternal`. Codex device-auth URL now opens Safari (or the user's default), not a fresh Electron webContents.
- **Picker freeze mid-session** (commit `93dc530`). `pickDriver` returns early when `sessionId` is set. Per-pane `toggleDd` and reveal-view `fpToggleDd` also return early; clicking the chip mid-session is a no-op at all three layers.
- **AuthPromptCard persistence + retry layout** (commit `772915f`). `openSession` filters existing transcript for `kind === "AuthPrompt"` rows and carries them into the fresh transcript. Card stays pinned at the top across turns. Retry button flush-right on the exit-state row, on the same line as the status text.
- **Sprint 083 top-bar polish** (commit `772915f` traffic-light y=13 and earlier `e00885d`).
- **Gates.** typecheck ✓, lint ✓, build ✓. `dist/reveal.html` 43.29 kB gzipped; JS bundle 16.91 kB gzipped (under the 60 kB Phase 8 budget). No new signals; `signals/0.1.json` unchanged at 30 tags.
- **Files touched across the arc.** `server.py`, `web/vm/session_controller.ts`, `web/vm/types.ts`, `web/reveal.ts`, `web/reveal_component.ts`, `web/reveal.html`, `web/reveal/transcript/{AuthPromptCard,Row,useController}.tsx`, `web/reveal/transcript/ansi.ts`, `electron/main.js`, `harness/shakeout/cli_discovery.ts`, `harness/shakeout/run.ts`, plus sprint cards for 083/084/085a/085b/085c (085c below).

- **2026-09-28 (Sprint 089, MEDIUM) — external-runtime deps declared as `extra` in an upstream wheel but imported unconditionally by a reachable code path.** The `httpx` case: `substrate-kernel 1.1.0`'s `Requires-Dist` puts `httpx` under `[openai-compat]` and `[dev]`, and `substrate/adapters/models.py:345` does an unconditional `import httpx` on the first live-driver turn's context-token probe. `pip install substrate-kernel==1.1.0` alone gives a runtime that raises `ModuleNotFoundError` the moment a real driver talks. Static tests don't catch it (`substrate` itself has httpx in `[dev]` so its own suite runs green); mypy passes; ruff passes; the packaged smoke found it. **Watch pattern:** any time `scripts/fetch-python-runtime.sh` upgrades `substrate-kernel` or any bundled wheel, cross-check the wheel's `Requires-Dist` against `grep -rn '^import\|^from' <package>/` for any dep listed only under an extra. **Re-visit condition:** substrate-kernel's next version bump, OR a new bundled dep, OR any packaged-smoke run that surfaces a fresh `ModuleNotFoundError`. **Cheap escalation:** promote the check into a step inside `fetch-python-runtime.sh` itself (post-install: `python -c "import substrate.adapters.models"` and any other module the code path can reach — a compile-time import check catches the class before the packaged smoke gets a turn to see it).

- **2026-09-28 (post-compact drift, MEDIUM) — compact-summary values that look-like-a-known-format can be hallucinated.** The Sep 28 session compaction recorded `APPLE_API_ISSUER = ce7e5b05-e5a8-4836-b61d-aa5794eeb3f4`; the value is a plausible UUID that authenticates 401 against Apple's notary. The correct value is `ce7e5b05-566f-4024-bdfc-e7de968b3218` — the compaction kept the first block and hallucinated the last three, plus reused the trailing bytes from Apple's own doc-example UUID (`c055ca8c-e5a8-4836-b61d-aa5794eeb3f4`). **Watch pattern:** any UUID, hash, key ID, or file-path in a compact summary that names a system-external identity (Apple issuer, GitHub token, DB dsn, PyPI token). **Re-visit condition:** before re-using any such value from a compact summary, verify it against the primary source — a live tool call, the actual .env, the current transcript search — not against another summary. **Cheap escalation:** the Sprint 089 D-section could hard-code the issuer UUID into `WORKING_AGREEMENT.md § External SDK bridge mappings` under "App Store Connect notary" so the value has a canonical home the summary can never mangle.

- **2026-09-28 (diagnosis discipline, HIGH) — pattern-matching a diff as a root cause without checking what the failing code path reads.** Twice in one hour on Sprint 089 today: first the "packaged runs older substrate than source" claim (wrong — same commit); second the "records/ci_mode.record/blobs+sidecar missing from bundle → that's the Structure bug" claim (a real amputation, but the causal link to "no topology loaded" was fabricated, and blobs/sidecar are demo-fixture assets, not runtime state — a category error). Both stemmed from finding a plausible difference and naming it "the cause" before opening the client code and tracing which endpoint the failing pane calls. **Watch pattern:** any surfaced defect whose stated cause is a diff between two configurations rather than a trace from the failing UI element down through the network call down to the code that services the call. **Cheap escalation:** the standing rule "read the code; grep repeated literals" — extend it: "for any UI-visible bug, first move is to grep the client for the string the UI displays, find the code that emits it, find the network call whose failure/absence triggers it, and only then compare configurations." That order was inverted twice today. Ties to the earlier "be your own skeptic" and "external-check-surface" entries — a green pass is not a proof, and a plausible diff is not a cause.

- **2026-09-28 (packaging discipline, HIGH) — "the smoke is green" does not prove "the packaged app is a mechanical translation of source mode."** `packaged_app_smoke.ts` verifies one narrow slice: a typed literal reaches the transcript DOM against the deterministic driver. It does not exercise Reveal panes (Structure, Records, Studio, Assay), the summoned-surface layer, driver-picker CLI probes, workspace binding to any path other than `~/.substrate/sandbox`, session resume, streaming tool progress, delegate fan-out, or any of the twenty-plus other user-visible surfaces the reveal shell carries. The Architect's first live use of the notarized `.app` found Structure showing "no topology loaded" while chat works — a class of bug the smoke by design cannot see. **Watch pattern:** any claim that "the packaged app works" grounded only on the packaged smoke passing. **Cheap escalation:** the packaged smoke gates NOT-A-MECHANICAL-TRANSLATION as a failure mode. The gate that closes the class is a source-vs-packaged behavior parity harness — the shakeout suite's nine Axis-A user flows run twice per PR, once against source-mode `npm run electron`, once against the packaged binary, with diff-to-zero on the emitted signal traces. Anything the shakeout catches in source mode but not in packaged mode is a packaging bug filed as a Sprint 089 follow-on. Nothing has closed this gate as of 2026-09-28.

- **2026-09-28 (BLACKBOARD is agent continuity, not user report), HIGH — stop writing to `## Surfaced for review` as narration.** The kit's `## Surfaced for review` is for typed halts and Rubber Duck observations marked `surfaced`. It is not a place to narrate diagnostic steps to the Architect in prose. Continuity across sessions lives in `## Built`, `## Sprint tail`, `## Drift watchlist`. Standing directive from the Architect this session: *"Use the blackboard as an intermediary. It's not our intermediary; it's your intermediary over time."* Postmortem § 7.

- **2026-09-28 (no notarize without user-observable verification, HIGH) — notarize is the last step, not the mechanical next step.** Twice this session I kicked off `npm run dist` before running the packaged binary through the surfaces the user cares about (Reveal, Structure, chat with a real model, window lifecycle). Every review-fix is verifiable against a signed-not-notarized copy launched from `/tmp`; notarization adds Apple's Accepted status, not evidence the fixes work. **Rule**: no notarize submission until the local signed-not-notarized copy has passed source-vs-packaged parity on Structure + one real-model turn + Cmd-W → Dock-click. Postmortem § 8.

- **2026-09-28 (test-driver default, HIGH) — real models over deterministic in every smoke.** The Architect's standing rule this session: *"the deterministic thing has always led to serious issues. Just use real models."* `packaged_app_smoke.ts` uses `deterministic`. Real-model turns exercise the CLI-driver PATH path, the httpx import path, streaming, tool round-trips, and the Structure pane's live topology-graph fetch under realistic latency. Deterministic short-circuits every one. **Re-visit condition**: every smoke, every shakeout flow, every parity test. Deterministic only when the test genuinely requires bit-determinism. **Cheap escalation**: `harness/shakeout/lib/` grows a default-driver constant that resolves to a locally-available real model (`llama3.2:1b` on Ollama, or `qwen2.5-coder:7b`) unless overridden. Postmortem § 9.

- **2026-09-28 (diagnosis order for UI-visible bugs, HIGH) — grep the client for the failing string before doing anything else.** For any user-visible bug: (1) grep the client for the string the UI displays, (2) find the component that emits it, (3) find the network call whose failure or absence triggers it, (4) run that call against source-mode and packaged-mode daemons, (5) compare responses. Only then compare configurations. Twice today on the Structure symptom I inverted this order and named plausible-looking diffs as causes (older-substrate; missing blobs/sidecar); both were wrong. A diff between two configurations is not an external check surface under Rubber Duck Pass discipline (Huang 2023: pure intrinsic self-critique degrades reasoning). **Re-visit condition**: every session-start ritual should re-anchor this order before the first diagnosis. Postmortem § 2, § 3.

- **2026-09-28 (packaging sprint gate, HIGH) — a packaging sprint closes on source-vs-packaged behavior parity, not on codesign + notary + one-literal-smoke.** Sprint 089 was called green on codesign verifies + spctl accepts + stapler validates + notary Accepted + `packaged_app_smoke` exits 0. The Architect opened the notarized `.app`, clicked Reveal → Structure on a live session, and it says "no topology loaded." The sprint's gate proved bundle integrity + one narrow round-trip; it did not prove the packaged binary reproduces the source-mode app's behavior. Rule: every packaging sprint's observation contract must run the nine Axis-A shakeout flows against BOTH `npm run electron` and the packaged binary, with the emitted signal traces diffed-to-zero where the flows overlap. Anything the shakeout catches in source mode but not in packaged mode is a packaging bug that gates the sprint. **Re-visit condition**: every packaging change (fetch script, main.js env, electron-builder config, Info.plist, entitlements, asarUnpack). **Cheap escalation**: `harness/shakeout/run.ts` grows a `SHAKEOUT_TARGET=source|packaged` env, and the sprint card's Section F declares which flows must diff-to-zero. Postmortem: `process/planning/POSTMORTEM-2026-09-28-sdd-failure-modes-sprint-089-packaging.md § 1`.

- **2026-09-28 — Workspace list picks up pytest tmpdirs and other test-produced paths.** Observed live: a recent-workspace surface showed `/private/var/folders/…/T/pytest-of-<user>/pytest-<n>/test_sse_stream_reads_turn_eve0` as a bindable workspace. That is a pytest tmpdir, produced by the test harness through the standard `tmp_path` / `tmpdir` fixture, and it must never surface in the workspace list. **Do not** solve this by hard-coding the specific paths. Solve it by recognising the SHAPE of test-produced paths and excluding at the source: any path under `$TMPDIR` or `/private/var/folders/`, any component matching pytest's tmpdir convention (`pytest-of-<user>/pytest-<n>/…` or `tmpXXXXXXXX/`), the `xdist_worker_slot_` / `session_scope_` markers pytest-xdist leaves, and any path whose first component is `/tmp`, `/private/tmp`, or the current-user's `TMPDIR`. The recognition belongs on the write side (whatever code records a workspace path also declares the classifier that rejects a test-tmpdir shape) so a future test framework's tmpdir shape gets caught by the same gate. Likely files that touch this: `server.py` `_load_recent_workspaces` / `_save_recent_workspace` / `_workspace_shape` and the classifier `_classify_workspace_shape` around the workspace-recent LRU. **Re-visit condition**: any next workspace-list surface change. Not a packaging bug; a general defect. **Never** hard-code path exclusions by name — the rule is by class of artifact. Current behavior: typing at the prompt does not pin the view to the bottom, so a fresh user message can appear below the fold and require manual scroll to see. Model output usually pins; tool output sometimes does not. **Target**: standard terminal-style pinning. While the view is at the bottom, every new content event — user typing, streaming model reply, streaming tool progress, ToolResult, final answer — pins the view to the bottom. The user scrolls up: the pin releases; new content appends without moving the view; the "new content below" indicator (if any) becomes visible. The user scrolls back to the bottom: the pin re-engages. This is `less +F` / iTerm / VS Code terminal semantics. Files that likely touch it: `web/reveal/transcript/*`, `web/reveal_component.ts` at the pane-scroll wiring. **Re-visit condition**: next UI polish pass after the packaging sprint stabilises. Not a packaging bug — a shell UX defect independent of Sprint 089.

#### Sprint 089 (2026-09-28, earlier attempt — signed+notarized artifact + smoke passed against deterministic driver only) — first close was called on the wrong gate
- **Reopen reason.** The Architect opened `dist-electron/Substrate-1.1.0-arm64.dmg` on first live inspection. Chat works against a loaded topology. Reveal → Structure says "no topology loaded" — a bug the source-mode `npm run electron` does not exhibit. The sprint was called green on the codesign/notary/stapler/smoke gate; the gate does not prove packaged-vs-source parity. See ## Surfaced for review 2026-09-28 for the diagnostic fumbles and the honest state of the diagnosis. This close-entry stays here as history; the sprint itself is not closed.

  - **Notary path cleared.** Corrected the issuer UUID (compact-summary drift, filed in ## Surfaced for review + ## Drift watchlist) and canonicalised the three non-secret identifiers into `WORKING_AGREEMENT.md § External SDK bridge mappings`. `xcrun notarytool history` now returns the submission list; a new `npm run dist` produced `dist-electron/mac-arm64/Substrate.app` (notarize successful, stapler validates, spctl reports `accepted, source=Notarized Developer ID`) and `dist-electron/Substrate-1.1.0-arm64.dmg` (145 MB, contains the stapled .app). `.dmg` is not itself signed/stapled — electron-builder's `mac.notarize: true` submits the .app; the ticket lives on the .app inside the .dmg; Gatekeeper on the target Mac reads it there. Truly-offline first-open via a signed .dmg is a separate follow-on.

Notary status → `Accepted`. `npm run smoke:packaged` against the notarized bundle → `ok — typed packaged-smoke-mum4gty7 landed in input and appeared in transcript (main pid 20810)`.
- **Only Section E remains.** Move `.dmg` to a Mac that hasn't built the app (or a fresh user account with cleared Gatekeeper state), apply the quarantine xattr per Sprint 089 E1, open the .app from /Applications, click Open on the Gatekeeper dialog, watch a Substrate window paint, type a turn, watch the transcript render. That is the human's step; no agent work required.
- **Scope.** A signed .dmg on local disk that opens on a different Mac, per Apple's own docs. Full card at `process/sprints/sprint-089-mac-packaging-per-apple-rules.md`; Section F added today declares the observation contract.
- **What landed today.** (1) `harness/shakeout/packaged_app_smoke.ts` + `npm run smoke:packaged` — packaged-binary observation harness with real-keyboard input against `input[placeholder^="type to talk"]`, stale-build refusal, codesign team assertion, `process.resourcesPath` check, orphan-daemon sweep. (2) `scripts/fetch-python-runtime.sh` fixed twice: adds `httpx` to the pip install so the daemon's context-token probe doesn't raise; keeps `substrate/` loose in `site-packages/` (same treatment `msgspec/` already got) so the role-prompt loader's `Path(substrate.__file__).parent / "topologies/session/prompts/default.md"` resolves on disk. Bundle: 940 files, 37M. (3) `electron-builder.config.js` unchanged from Sprint 089's Round 1; `electron/main.js` unchanged; `build/entitlements.mac.plist` unchanged. (4) `package.json` gains a single new script `smoke:packaged` (not wired into `shakeout` or `electron:smoke` — packaged smoke needs a fresh signed build and refuses to run against a stale one).
- **Dual contract.** *Signal* — vacuous; the harness is TypeScript around the packaged binary, no new controller emits. *Artifact* — the four files above have non-zero size, `npm run smoke:packaged` returns exit 0, `codesign --display --verbose=2` reports `TeamIdentifier=ZVL8XB9XGU`. *Observation* — Section F executable form. Verified 2026-09-28: `packaged_app_smoke: ok — typed packaged-smoke-mum14k8i landed in input and appeared in transcript (main pid 72502)`.
- **Rubber Duck Pass.**
  - *Sequence narration.* Direct `Contents/MacOS/Substrate` launch: `[electron] spawning server: <bundled python3> <asar-unpacked server.py> --port 0`. `[server] substrate-ui port=<ephemeral>`. `[electron] server bound port=<ephemeral>`. `[electron] server up on http://127.0.0.1:<ephemeral>`. `[server] boot_scan: 3114 manifest(s) in ~5.7s`. On `POST /api/session` deterministic driver: 200 with `session_id`, role `default`, workspace at `~/.substrate/sessions/<id>/workspace`. Under `packaged_app_smoke`: firstWindow → mount observer fires → pane 1 bound → transcript mount `[data-vm-atom-root="terminal"]` appears → `openSession` returns → real keyboard typing → literal in DOM.

    - **Vocabulary gap 1** — httpx missing from the bundled runtime (Finding 1 above). Disposition: `resolved-here` via `scripts/fetch-python-runtime.sh` edit.
    - **Vocabulary gap 2** — `substrate` package in `_bundle.zip` breaks the role-prompt loader (Finding 2 above). Disposition: `resolved-here` via the same script.

The `[electron] spawning server: ...` log line reliably appears when the binary is launched directly (verified via a raw `Contents/MacOS/Substrate` invocation), but does not always reach `app.process().stderr` before `firstWindow()` resolves. The smoke's `spawning server:` assertion was a false negative in its first version; replaced with a post-close scan of the captured stream + a `ModuleNotFoundError` scan. Disposition: `resolved-here`. Related: Addendum A rule A4 ("verify the observer, fix races at root").
    - **Timing surprise** — the transcript mount briefly logs `transcript root mounted (terminal, pane 1)` at first render, then unmounts because pane 1 is unbound (the reveal shell shows the path-binding input instead). `pane_prompt_isolation.ts` handles this by walking the React fiber to `logic._bindPane`; the packaged smoke now does the same. Disposition: `resolved-here`.

#### Sprint 089 (2026-09-28, STILL OPEN — review applied, ten fixes landed on the signed build, Structure symptom NOT verified fixed, postmortem filed) — the sprint's own gate proved the wrong properties
- **Postmortem.** `process/planning/POSTMORTEM-2026-09-28-sdd-failure-modes-sprint-089-packaging.md`. Eleven failure modes mapped to SDD discipline, two concrete process changes queued (parity gate, Rubber Duck Pass discipline before pattern-match), one product feature filed under `## Deferred` (transcript auto-scroll pinning).
- **Review applied.** `process/reviews/REVIEW-2026-09-28-packaging-vs-electron-standard.md`. Ten fixes landed: F1 (`RUNS` → `~/.substrate/runs/`), F2 (`restoreShellPath` in `main.js`), F5 (`protocols` block in `electron-builder.config.js` → `CFBundleURLTypes`), F6 (`window-all-closed` on macOS no longer kills the server), F7 (log at `~/Library/Logs/Substrate/substrate.log` + `dialog.showErrorBox` on startup failure), F9 (KILL_GRACE_MS 3s → 45s), F10 (`__pycache__` swept from bundle + `CFBundleVersion` = epoch-seconds, unique per build), plus `app.setName("Substrate")` so `app.getPath` uses the product name, plus `WEB.mkdir` guarded against EPERM. Looked in: ... .../_bundle.zip/substrate/topologies/session/prompts/`. The role-prompt loader resolves `Path(substrate.__file__).parent / "topologies/session/prompts/default.md"`; `pathlib.Path` cannot descend into a zip archive. Same class as msgspec's existing loose placement (msgspec keeps `.so` extension modules that `zipimport` cannot dlopen). Fix: `scripts/fetch-python-runtime.sh` now keeps `substrate/` + `substrate_kernel-*.dist-info/` loose in `site-packages/` alongside `msgspec/`. Zip retains the rest of the pure-Python deps. The correct UUID sits in this session's own transcript on disk (`APPLE_API_ISSUER = ce7e5b05-566f-4024-bdfc-e7de968b3218`) — the compaction preserved the first block and hallucinated the last three. `xcrun notarytool history` with the corrected UUID returned a full submission list, including a prior submission from earlier today (2026-09-28 22:25:58Z, id `bc87be86-606b-4016-a7c5-8b06c4572cdf`) with status `Accepted`. **Standing lesson (drift watchlist candidate):** compact-summary values that name-check like known formats (UUIDs, hashes, IDs) can look right and be wrong; treat any value the summary claims verbatim as unverified until re-derived from primary source. - **2026-09-28 Claude — Sprint 089 REOPENED. The packaged .app is signed and notarized but is NOT a mechanical translation of source mode. It behaves differently from `npm run electron` in at least one user-visible way already found on first live inspection, and the smoke I called "green" only proved the narrowest slice (a typed literal round-trips through the transcript DOM against a deterministic driver). "Same app" was the promise; the .app does not deliver on it.**
  - **User-visible divergence found on first inspection.** The Architect opened the notarized `.app` from `dist-electron/Substrate-1.1.0-arm64.dmg`, loaded a topology, was talking to it. Chat works. Then clicked Reveal → Structure. The Structure pane says "no topology loaded" while a live session is running against a loaded topology. Source-mode `npm run electron` does not exhibit this. Cause not yet traced to code.
  - **Where I fumbled the diagnosis, on the record.** First swing I claimed the packaged daemon was running an older substrate than source mode. Wrong — `git rev-list v1.1.0..HEAD -- src/ = 0`, source-mode's editable install and the PyPI 1.1.0 wheel are the same code. Second swing I diffed the two substrate site-packages trees and found `topologies/*/records/ci_mode.record/blobs/` and `.../sidecar/` missing from the bundled tree. I framed this as "the packaging bug" without checking whether the Structure pane actually reads those paths. The Architect caught the category error: `records/ci_mode.record/` is a SHIPPED demo fixture, not runtime state; the Structure pane's "no topology loaded" complaint is about the CURRENTLY RUNNING topology's live introspection, a different class of thing. The blobs/sidecar amputation may or may not be a real packaging bug (it is — files that ship in the source tree are missing from the bundle) but it is not automatically THE bug behind the Structure complaint. Two swings, two pattern-matches, both premature.
  - **What actually differs between source mode and packaged mode, from evidence gathered.**
    - Source mode: `uv run python <substrate-ui>/server.py --port 0` with `cwd=<substrate>`, editable-install substrate from `<substrate>/src/substrate/`, uv-managed venv.
    - Packaged mode: `<app>/Contents/Resources/python/bin/python3 <app>/.../app.asar.unpacked/server.py --port 0` with `cwd=<app>/Contents/Resources`, substrate loose in bundled `site-packages/`, stdlib in `python313.zip`, most pure-python deps in `_bundle.zip`.
    - `server.py` byte-identical in both cases (checked).
    - `substrate/src/` and bundled `substrate/` diff: only `__pycache__` differences AND `topologies/*/records/ci_mode.record/{blobs,sidecar}/` present in source, ABSENT in bundle. That is real package-data amputation happening somewhere in the fetch-runtime → pip-wheel-shipping → zip-site-packages pipeline. Whether the pipeline strips it or the PyPI wheel never had it needs one more check (bundled python has no pip, cannot introspect the wheel directly; need a separate wheel unzip).
    - Working directory differs. Any code path that resolves relative to `Path.cwd()` diverges.
    - The `~/.substrate/` scan is 3114 manifests in both modes — same user home. That is NOT a packaged-vs-source difference. My earlier surfacing about "the app scans my home directory as if it were app state" was orthogonal to the immediate bug — a real distribution question, not the Structure diagnosis.
  - **What I still have not done, honestly named.** I have not opened the reveal-shell client code and traced which endpoint the Structure pane calls, nor run that endpoint against the packaged daemon and source-mode daemon side-by-side to compare responses. That is the real diagnosis. Everything above is upstream of it.
  - **Where the packaging stands.** Signed. Notarized. Stapled onto the `.app`. `.dmg` on disk. `spctl accepted, source=Notarized Developer ID`. `packaged_app_smoke` green against a deterministic driver. The Architect demonstrated a real user-visible bug on first inspection. Not a done sprint.

#### Sprint 089 (2026-09-29 — resuming ended sessions fixed; config-externalization review verified)
- **Resume fix: in source, `dist-electron` and `/Applications`.** Reported: pick an ended session up, the old transcript shows, typing starts a new session. Three shared-code causes, the same in both builds:
  - **Server, session end rule.** `session_topology` finalises on `threshold_count(SessionEnded, 1)`, and the runtime counts the whole record on resume. A resumed ended session finalised right after the new `UserMessage` (measured: `final_seq` 45 → 50, no model run). `server.py` `_with_session_end_threshold` now installs the threshold at prior `SessionEnded` count + 1.
  - **Client.** The `SessionEnded` handler called `forceClose`, clearing `sessionId`, so `sendTurn` opened a new session. `closeStreamOnEnd` keeps the session bound, and `sendTurn` reopens the stream before posting to an ended session. An explicit `endSession` still unbinds.
  - **Server, event stream.** `_session_events` closed on any `RunFinalised`, including one at or before `since_seq`. Only a `RunFinalised` past the cursor ends the stream now. The piece-B finding-2 test was rewritten to the new contract.
- **Evidence.** New harness `harness/shakeout/resume_ended_session.ts` (temp `HOME`): end, re-attach, type. It passes in source, `dist-electron` and `/Applications`, each with the same session ID, replies `["ok","yes"]` and status `parked`. Full server suite: 18 failed and 188 passed, identical with and without the change. The 18 failures predate it.
- **Installed.** `/Applications/Substrate.app` is build 1790667186. The previous build is in the Trash as `Substrate-1790661508-replaced-20260929-003716.app`.
- **Orphan backend. Written, untested, not in the installed build.** Pid 38409 was a backend from an earlier build, parent `launchd`, listening on port 62149. The parent-death watchdog `print`s to the dead Electron pipe, and the `BrokenPipeError` kills it before shutdown. `server.py` now routes shutdown lines through `_say`, which ignores `OSError`, and the watchdog calls `os._exit(0)` after shutdown. The parent-death test was stopped before it ran. Pid 38409 was left running.
- **Review verified.** `process/reviews/REVIEW-2026-09-29-configuration-externalization-the-missing-packaging-step-RESPONSE.md`:
  - The diagnosis is correct for F1–F4, the source/packaged divergence. It does not cover the Structure, resume, orphan and F6 failures, which are shared-code defects.
  - Ten citation and code errors are listed, among them the role-prompt row (the shipped prompts resolve relative to the package) and "Ardito et al." (actually Ghammam et al.).
  - One claim withdrawn: shared `~/.substrate` is by design, one app per machine.
- **To do, recorded in that response.** `substrate_home()` / `$SUBSTRATE_HOME` so `npm run electron` uses `~/.substrate-dev`. It covers 22 hard-coded sites and needs `substrate-kernel` 1.1.1.
- **Not started.** The pre-packaging worktree at `85e5b11` (substrate `18aef5a0`).

#### Sprint 092 (2026-10-01, IN PROGRESS — blob externalization, EnvelopeKind fixes, six UI polish items landed in source; workspace pollution investigation open)

**Three sprints of source work completed (A, B, C). TypeScript compiles clean. Vite build succeeds. 359 substrate tests pass (1 pre-existing skip). 188 substrate-ui tests pass (18 pre-existing failures, confirmed identical on baseline). Assets copied into the packaged app at `dist-electron/mac-arm64/Substrate.app/Contents/Resources/app.asar.unpacked/`. The Architect then opened the real packaged app and reported two defects: workspace list still polluted with pytest tmpdirs, and startup too slow.**

- **Sprint A — sequencer blob externalization.** `_maybe_offload()` in `substrate/src/substrate/sequencer.py` replaces payloads >16 KiB with `{"$blob": "sha256:<hex>", "bytes": N}` stubs on the disk record. The function now returns `(disk_payload, memory_payload)` so triggers see the real data while the record gets the stub. Fixes session-killing OOM on large ToolCall payloads.

- **Sprint B — server.py SSE blob deref.** The SSE `/events` endpoint dereferences blob stubs before sending to the client, so the reveal shell receives full payloads.

- **Sprint C — client-side handlers + six UI polish items.**
  1. **EnvelopeKind values fixed.** `web/vm/kinds.ts` had wrong bare values for lifecycle kinds: `RunFinalised: "RunFinalised"` never matched `"substrate.RunFinalised"` on the record. Fixed `RunFinalised` → `"substrate.RunFinalised"`, `ProducerFailed` → `"substrate.ProducerFailed"`, added `InputBuildFailed: "substrate.InputBuildFailed"`.
  2. **InputBuildFailed handler.** `session_controller.ts` gains a case that renders `! input build failed: <error>` as a warning row.
  3. **RunFinalised orphaned-tool-call handler.** Detects the last ToolCall with no matching ToolResult and renders `! tool call <name> never returned — the run ended`.
  4. **Textarea prompt.** `reveal.html` `<input>` replaced with `<textarea rows="1">`. Enter sends, Shift+Enter inserts newline. Auto-grows on input, clears imperatively on send.
  5. **Scroll anchor.** `useScrollAnchor` wired into `Transcript.tsx` + `Row.tsx`. Sticky-bottom terminal pinning.
  6. **Markdown table rendering.** `markdown.ts` parses pipe tables. `ModelReply.tsx` renders `<table>` with styled headers and cells.
  7. **Ctrl+C scope fix.** `reveal.ts` Ctrl+C handler extended to exclude `TEXTAREA` from the interrupt path (was only excluding `INPUT`).
  8. **Activity line.** `reveal_component.ts` rest text appends `ctrl+c to stop` during live activity.

- **Files touched.** `web/vm/kinds.ts`, `web/vm/session_controller.ts`, `web/reveal/transcript/Transcript.tsx`, `web/reveal/transcript/Row.tsx`, `web/reveal.html`, `web/reveal_component.ts`, `web/reveal.ts`, `web/reveal/markdown.ts`, `web/reveal/transcript/ModelReply.tsx`.

- **Errors reported by the Architect on the live packaged app.**
  1. **Workspace list still polluted with pytest tmpdirs.** The Architect pasted the workspace list showing dozens of `/private/var/folders/.../pytest-385/...` through `pytest-442/...` paths from test runs. This is the same defect filed under `## Deferred` on 2026-09-28. Sprint 092's `tests/conftest.py` sets `SUBSTRATE_HOME` at session scope (autouse), but the isolation is failing. Investigation in progress — initial finding: `import server` at `test_server.py:25` runs at collection time BEFORE fixtures execute; `_SESSION_REGISTRY` is `None` at module level (`server.py:89`) and assigned inside `main()` at line 3991, so the import alone does not trigger registry init. The `base` fixture (module scope, line 32) creates `ThreadingHTTPServer` in-process. The question is where exactly SUBSTRATE_HOME is read relative to fixture execution, and whether some code path in the test harness writes workspace entries to `~/.substrate/` despite the isolation. Root cause not yet identified.
  2. **Startup too slow.** The Architect noted the app takes too long to start. `main.js` startup budget: `restoreShellPath` (~50ms) → `spawnServer` → `waitForPortReadback` (5s timeout) → `pollHealth` (15s timeout). Boot scan walks ~3114 manifests. Investigation deferred behind the workspace pollution fix.

- **Not committed.** All source changes are in the working tree per the batch-commits rule.

#### Review of Sprints 086b–092 (2026-10-01) — bugs re-filed as instances of seven practice classes
- **Technique: appeal to prior authority.** Each bug was matched to the established practice it violates, and that practice's canonical source was fetched and quoted. The source is the external check surface; the bug list is checked against it, not against memory. Roadmap: `process/planning/ROADMAP-2026-10-01-engineering-practice-classes.md`.
- **Gaps found, measured.**
  - (A/B) A test run under a throwaway `HOME` wrote 30 session dirs + 1 run record to the real `~/.substrate`: `RUNS`, `_SESSIONS_BASE` and `_SESSIONS_BASE_DEFAULT` read `SUBSTRATE_HOME` at import, before the Sprint 092 conftest sets it. `recent-workspaces.json` holds 61 deleted pytest tmpdirs out of 64 rows.
  - (C) `/Applications` build 1790667186 predates 090–092. `dist-electron` fails `codesign --verify` after the hand-copy. The drift guard counts commits, not a dirty `src/`. Sprint 091 is marked done with no `shakeout:packaged` script.
  - (D) The server stayed alive 31 s after SIGTERM with zero sessions: `srv.shutdown()` runs on the serving thread, so every quit waits for the 45 s SIGKILL. `main.js` `whenReady` sits outside the single-instance `else`. The orphan watchdog works (exits in 1 s).
  - (E) `kinds.ts` held unprefixed lifecycle kinds from Sep 23 to Oct 1. The ESLint guard misses `case` literals, and three lifecycle kinds have no handler.
  - (F) Sprint A resolves blob stubs for live views only. The resume fold (`_as_event`, `kernel/runtime.py`) still feeds stubs. Sprint B imports kernel internals into `server.py`.
  - (G) The 18 failing substrate-ui tests are classified by cause in the roadmap. The kernel has 1 failure (`test_assay_coding.py`), and its full run exceeded 30 min.
- **Order:** A+B → D → F → E → G → C. Each step closes on a named command check, not on reading.

#### Sprint 094 (2026-10-01, earlier pass, superseded by the entry above) — disposability (roadmap class D)
- **Shutdown deadlock fixed.** `_sigterm_handler` now starts `_shutdown_sequence` on a worker thread. SIGTERM to exit: 0.20 s with zero sessions (was alive 31 s later), 0.95 s with five parked real-model sessions (`ended=5`).
- **Orphans: class fixed.** The watchdog watched only its direct parent; in source mode that is `uv`, which outlives Electron. Electron now passes `SUBSTRATE_PARENT_PID`; the backend exits ~3 s after app death with `uv` in between. 15 orphaned backends from Sep 28–30 (incl. pid 38409) were stopped; 24 of 26 processes ignored SIGTERM (old code) and took SIGKILL. The live `/Applications` backend was left alone.
- **Single-instance:** `whenReady` returns when the lock was not acquired (Electron's documented shape).
- **Open:** startup time unmeasured (card `process/sprints/sprint-094-disposability.md`). Suite: 192 passed, same 18 failures. Not committed.

#### Sprint 094 (2026-10-01, closed in source mode) — startup measured and fixed; quit hang root-caused
- **Startup measured** against a clone of real state (3,079 sessions). The window and prompt were fast (0.8–1.3 s). The session list took 7.5–9.1 s: `boot_scan` read every record to precompute `next_turn_index` (6.9 of 7.1 s). Now derived on first use. Boot scan 0.20 s; list complete at 0.79 s.
- **Quit hang root-caused.** The interim handler (Event.set + Thread.start inside the SIGTERM handler) deadlocked on 2 of 9 runs. A native sample showed the main thread parked in the handler's lock wait. Python's signal docs forbid locks in handlers. Replaced with a self-pipe; quit to backend gone 0.50–0.93 s on 6 of 6 runs.
- **Real-use test.** `packaged_app_smoke.ts` now times each stage and runs against `SMOKE_STATE` (it used to write the real `~/.substrate`). Two source runs: real-model turn parked, Structure populated, quit with a live session 0.85–0.87 s.
- Full table: `process/sprints/sprint-094-disposability.md`. Suite: 193 passed, same 18 failures. Packaged target unmeasured until sprint 098.

The resume fold, `view_at`, the session transcript, delegate, parent context, `inspect_record` and the turn-index scan read resolved; integrity readers stay raw. The record bytes are unchanged.
- **Proven:** the new test failed before the change (resumed View held a stub, giving `substrate.PredicateQuarantined`) and passes after. Over HTTP, a 30,000-char payload is a stub on disk and arrives whole from `/api/records/<name>` and the SSE stream.

- **UI:** `server.py` now redeems through the kernel; its private blob reader and the two kernel-internal imports are gone.

### Sprint 101 (2026-10-02, closed) — agent work is not a short request (class H, new)
- Removed default wall-clock limits on model work (turn, delegate, CLI waits, Ollama and CLI calls); `num_predict` defaults to `num_ctx`. bash: per-call `timeout_s` (120/600), process-group kill on deadline and cancel, separate stderr reader, background children no longer block. Failure states: manifest `running` during a turn; a failed turn's status and index from the record; a caller's timeout parks the turn; shutdown interrupts running turns; the activity strip shows failed turns as ended (`web/reveal/activity.ts`). Tool calls keyed `callId@seq`. `test:unit` gates releases.
- Gates: kernel 1,210 / 3 skipped; UI 215; client specs 20/20; realmodel 40/41 (ensemble agreement, stochastic, 5/5 on rerun). Card: `process/sprints/sprint-101-agent-work-is-not-a-short-request.md`.

### Sprint 102 (2026-10-02, closed) — transcript scroll, and the class H sweep
- Scroll: `useScrollAnchor(key)` follows the bottom while the user is there, holds the row being read once they scroll up, resumes at the bottom, and keeps its place per pane per view. Gate `transcript_follow.ts` drives the Electron app (7/7; the old hook fails 3, with a 973 px gap) and runs in `release.sh`.
- Class H sweep, each a state with no exit or a cancel that could not reach the work: CLI drivers now die with the interrupt (process group); delegate children stop when the parent is interrupted (`_TOOL_CANCEL_HOOKS`); a stream reconnect no longer appends the transcript again; a background topology run whose worker died reports `failed`, not `running`; a backend death after startup shows a dialog with Relaunch or Quit (lifecycle F8).
- Gates: kernel 1,212 / 3 skipped; UI 216; client specs 21/21; realmodel 41/41. Card: `process/sprints/sprint-102-scroll-and-the-class-h-sweep.md`.

### Sprint 103 (2026-10-02, closed) — supervised background commands (Claude Code parity)
- `bash(cmd, timeout_s?, run_in_background?)` writes to files, never pipes. Background returns a task id at once. A foreground command still running at its deadline moves to the background (a `sleep` is killed). Leftover children of a finished shell become a task. `bash_output`, `bash_stop` and `bash_tasks` read, stop and list the session's tasks. Tasks stop when a delegated child ends, when the session ends or is deleted, and when the app quits; output over 5 GB is killed. The bash schema now lets native tool calls pass `timeout_s`; it could not since sprint 101.
- Gates: kernel 1,221 / 3 skipped; UI 218; client specs 21/21; realmodel 42/42 (a kimi session ran, read, used and stopped a background web server, 3 of 3). Research `process/planning/RESEARCH-2026-10-02-background-commands.md`; roadmap `ROADMAP-2026-10-02-background-commands.md` (104 exit notice, 105 tasks in the app); card `process/sprints/sprint-103-supervised-background-commands.md`.

### Sprint 104 (2026-10-02, closed) — the model hears when a background task ends
- A task that ends without the model stopping it is written to the record as `BackgroundTaskEnded` before the next model step, and that step's prompt carries `[background task bg_… (cmd) exited 0; last output: …]`. While parked, the notice waits for the next turn's first step. Each ending is told once. The app shows it as a row.
- Gates: kernel 1,225 / 2 skipped; UI 218; client specs 22/22; realmodel 43/43 (kimi named a background task's output from the notice alone, 3 of 3). Card `process/sprints/sprint-104-the-model-hears-when-a-task-ends.md`.

### Sprint 105 (2026-10-02, closed) — background tasks in the app
- The activity strip lists each running background task (`task bg_… · cmd · runtime · stop`) in both views; stop kills it and the model hears about it. Daemon: `GET /api/session/<id>/tasks`, `POST …/tasks/<task_id>/stop`. Gate `tasks_gate.ts` drives a real kimi turn in the Electron app and runs in `release.sh`.
- Found at release: `caret_pin` (a clicked card header moving) had failed 1 run in 3-4 since before sprint 102. A closing card shrank the content and the browser clamped scrollTop. The scroll hook now pins a clicked header and holds the transcript's height until the user scrolls: 9 of 9 clean.
- Found: the locked signal vocabulary has said 30 tags while listing 31 since sprint 087 (2026-09-27), and its parity check ran in no gate. Fixed the count; parity now gates releases. The background-commands roadmap (103–105) is complete.
- Gates: kernel 1,224 / 3 skipped; UI 220; client specs 23/23. Card `process/sprints/sprint-105-background-tasks-in-the-app.md`.

- **2026-10-02 user** — Agent work has no wall-clock limit. "models can work for some time … Claude Code is basically the standard. Nothing can work for 20 minutes, you know what I mean? It doesn't just like... you have to cancel it." No turn is cancelled for lack of progress either: "we're not canceling a turn after a long stretch with no progress. I already made that decision." A turn ends when it finishes or when the user interrupts it. Sprint 101.

- **2026-10-02 Claude — The transcript has not followed the bottom since 2026-09-24.** The Architect reported no sticky/free scrolling in build 1790925851. The 2026-09-15 behaviour (`83b20bd`: follow the tail only when already at the bottom; keep each pane's position across view switches) was deleted by Sprint 076 as "superseded by `useScrollAnchor`". Meanwhile the hook's own comments assign follow-bottom to the `reveal.ts` code 076 deleted, and the hook pins the topmost row forever. Sprint 075's sticky invariant never had a check; Sprint 092 recorded "Sticky-bottom terminal pinning" with no observation run; the 093–099 review did not re-verify it. No gate measures scroll position after a row arrives. Postmortem: `process/planning/POSTMORTEM-2026-10-02-transcript-follow-bottom-regression.md`. Said in chat the same turn.

- **2026-10-02 Claude — Class H: agent work modelled as a short request.** Turn 28 died at the daemon's 600 s cap. The model was not working: its bash call had started a background server that held the tool's output pipe, and the tool's read loop had no deadline. The cap removed the symptom and could not stop the thread. The strip kept counting under the red error, and the bash card showed turn 9's result because call ids restart each turn. Sprint 101: no wall-clock limits on model work, a real per-call bash deadline with process-group kill, failure states with exits (manifest, turn index, timeout parks, shutdown interrupts, the strip shows "ended"), calls keyed `callId@seq`, and the output cap raised to the context window (Ollama counts reasoning against it). Class doc: `process/planning/CLASS-2026-10-02-agent-work-modelled-as-a-short-request.md`. Said in chat the same turn.

### Kernel sprint 250 (2026-10-08, closed) — record integrity and the Claim Check
- The Claim Check covers injected events: a 2 MB resume event is a blob stub and the record reads back whole. A failed append consumes no seq.

### Sprint 111 (2026-10-08, closed) — server correctness and composition root
- `server.App` holds the 13 tables that were module globals; `main()` builds it, both listeners carry it, and `Handler.app` reads it. Tests build their own App (`app` fixture, `serving(app)`); the 37 assignments to server tables are gone, and two Apps served at once share nothing.
- The session's end rule moved into the kernel: `finalise_on(SessionEnded)` replaces `threshold_count(SessionEnded, 1)`, which a resume's restored counts tripped on the first new event. The server's copy of the rule and its per-turn whole-record scan are deleted; turn and end read the tail seq from the last frame.
- N004, a new defect: the role a session was created with never reached it, so no UI session had its role prompt (`default.md` included). Fixed, resolving against the session's workspace.
- One source for the port, the Ollama address and the Claude catalog (`claude-opus-5-5`, `claude-sonnet-5-5`). `/api/agent` refuses an unknown model instead of running the stub. `/api/records` caches rows by segment fingerprint. The boot scan runs once and cannot overwrite a session a request changed.
- Legacy and Studio endpoints deleted. 396 leaked runs moved from `~/.substrate/runs` to `~/.Trash/substrate-runs-leak-2026-10-08`.
- Gates: UI 210; vm_smoke 12/12; lifecycle, resume-after-end and tasks gates pass; kernel 1,271 passed / 4 skipped / 43 real-model deselected. Card: `process/sprints/sprint-111-server-correctness-composition-root.md`.

### Kernel sprint 251 (2026-10-08, halted on F017; every other row closed) — runtime liveness
- HALT, F017: each session turn rereads the whole record, so turn N costs O(N). Measured on 300 deterministic turns: 15.5 ms at the start, 351 ms by turn 200. Three passes per turn became one (`record.recover_and_read`; the registry reads only the first frame); turn 200 now takes 186 ms. The linear growth stays until the Architect picks a design: snapshot-able Views, or a session runtime kept paused in memory between turns.
- `quiescence_with_watchdog(seconds)` had no watchdog (`seconds` only capped a 10 ms poll); it is `quiescence()`, and the dead `watchdog_seconds` parameter left 13 topologies, two manifests and the console. `Budget.event_counts` is enforced: the over-cap emission is dropped and the Producer fails with `budget_exceeded`.
- `cancel_producer` returns the real parent; the active-runtime map keys on resolved paths; a factory that raises is refused at registration; `Subscription.matches` and `Event.from_envelope` are the one copy of their rules; `RunFailureReason` names the run-failure reasons.
- Found: `docs/api.md` had drifted 31 names behind (now regenerated, with a currency test); the record generator had committed a stray `pair_coding_chunked/` copy and left the real record stale (fixed; 18 records regenerated).
- Gates: kernel 1,279 passed / 4 skipped / 43 deselected; UI 210; vm_smoke, resume-after-end and lifecycle pass. Card: `substrate/process/sprints/sprint-251-runtime-liveness.md`.

- **2026-10-08 user** — "Oh, leaking test folders can be deleted. Don't worry about that. The best practices review that we're going to do next. You know, tests should be totally separate." Sprint 107: the test sessions and folders deleted; tests, gates and the app's test runs write only to their own temp dirs, and the release checks it.

- **2026-10-08 user** — The audit documents; it does not fix. "list findings only. You're not fixing things step by step ... you start fixing things before you even find a pattern ... What you do is you document everything ... You're not supposed to touch any code." Hold the kernel release; read through the lenses; record findings with sources; classify; roadmap; then sprints. Sprint 107's fixes stand as committed; the audit restarts as a read.

- **2026-10-08 Claude — The tests were not separate from the machine.** The real home held 3,094 test sessions and 1,380 empty test folders; `$TMPDIR` held 218 gate dirs; `~/Library` held 79 MB of test Electron profiles and logs; a model wrote demo files into the real `/tmp`; one UI test ran every installed CLI's model listing and rewrote `~/.cursor/cli-config.json`. The Python suites were already isolated; the gates, the app's Electron paths and that test were not. All deleted (sessions through the app's own endpoint), each cause fixed, and `release.sh` now refuses a release whose tests write into `HOME`. The same pass found the siblings Sprint 101 missed: `/end`, delete and every session setter waited behind a running turn, and glob/grep walked the whole disk where no interrupt could reach. Card `process/sprints/sprint-107-whole-project-pass.md`. Said in chat the same turn.

The agent fixed each defect the moment a test or gate run showed it (11 commits, about 30 fixes) and called that the pass. About 10–15% of the ~49,000 lines in scope was read closely; the rest got grep sweeps. No finding was classified, no source was quoted before a fix, and no roadmap preceded the code, so no pattern was named before code changed: the reverse of the method that produced `ROADMAP-2026-10-01-engineering-practice-classes.md`. Two findings remain unfixed and are recorded only: the kernel sdist ships 18,336 assay record files from `process/runs` (95 MB, 5 MB under PyPI's default upload limit, since 1.0.1); a session created with the default workspace stores the literal `~/.substrate/sandbox`, and the tools resolve it relative to the server's working directory (a literal `~` directory appeared in the kernel repo on 2026-09-27; one of the Architect's sessions carries this workspace). The kernel 1.1.2 release is held (version bumped and built; nothing uploaded). Next: the read, findings only, into a dated findings document, for the Architect's review before any roadmap or fix.

The Architect asked for a roadmap that addresses every finding, then the cards and execution, and retired dc-runtime "only if you don't break anything". Defaults taken where the Architect's answer is still open, each reversible: the coding bank grows to at least 160 problems; the Studio and legacy endpoints are deleted; the heavy SWE-bench topology is deleted. Order: gates that can fail (108) first, so every later fix is checked, then security (110), hermetic tests (109), the kernel sprints, the server (111), the renderer port (112, gated on 108's recorded baseline), packaging (113), and the 1.1.2 release.

### Sprint 114 (2026-10-09, closed) — a CLI driver runs in the session's workspace
- A `claude` session's CLI ran in the server's directory, not its workspace: under the installed app `/Applications/Substrate.app/Contents/Resources`, under the source build the `substrate` repo (it reported that repo's branch, commit and uncommitted files). `CliResponder` set no `cwd`.
- `CliResponder(cwd=)`; the server passes the session's workspace and keys its CLI responder cache on it, so two sessions on one CLI driver run in two directories.
- Live: a real `claude -p` turn in a per-session sandbox answered with that sandbox's path. Gates: kernel lint, mypy --strict, import contracts clean; UI 213. Card: `process/sprints/sprint-114-cli-driver-runs-in-the-session-workspace.md`.

### Sprint 115 (2026-10-09, closed) — every CLI call runs in its own folder
- Sprint 114 left two CLI callers in the wrong directory; both close here. A delegated child ran its CLI in the parent's workspace (fresh child) or the server's directory (`model=` child), and a nested delegate lost `model_resolver`. One-shot applications ran every CLI role in the server's directory.
- Kernel: `CliResponder.at(cwd)`; `_default_child_factory` makes the child workspace and runs a CLI responder there (`_in_workspace`); nested delegates keep `model_resolver`. Server: application builders take the run folder; code_review's roles run in `repo`; best_of_n_verified and research_sweep roles run in a temp folder removed when the run ends.
- Red before: the fresh child answered with the parent's folder, the `model=` child with the kernel repo; the app roles had no folder. Live with the real `claude -p`: both delegate paths answered with their own `delegate-runs/d1-cN/workspace`.
- Gates: kernel ruff, format, mypy --strict (138 files), lint-imports clean; kernel fast suite 1,328 passed / 5 skipped; UI 215 passed.

### UI U131 with kernel K286 (2026-10-10) — Substrate's tools stay Substrate's
- **What was wrong.** K284 recorded a harness driver's MCP call to Substrate's tools as `HarnessToolCall(via=mcp)` and ran it in the model step. The Architect caught it: that mixes the two tool sets and bypasses the tool producer.
- **K286.** The model step writes `ToolCall(origin=mcp)`. The tool producer runs it and writes `ToolResult(origin=mcp)`, and the session hands the result back over MCP. The continue-triggers skip `origin=mcp`, so no second CLI process starts. `HarnessToolCall` covers the harness's own tools only.
- **U131.** The rows read `(mcp)` and `(harness)`.
- **Gates.** Kernel 1,424 passed, UI 237 passed, Electron 11 of 11. In the installed app all three drivers made one model step each, with the pair written by the model and tool producers. Commits: kernel `309c7e2c`; UI `182ebe5` and the commit carrying this entry.
- **Open.** `electron_smoke` failed once on the first launch after an install, then passed twice (kit diary 66).

### Language in the app: U168–U172 with kernel K302–K313 (2026-10-13)
- **Kernel** (`substrate/process/planning/ROADMAP-2026-10-12-language-measurement-r3.md`, research round 5): a text-measurement library (`substrate.language`: preprocessing spec, published lexicons, 74 profile and 8 relation measures, Page–Hinkley drift, window minimums), the `substrate language <record>` command, and a language producer in the session topology that measures each reply between the final reply and `Returned` (44 ms first turn, 18 ms after, live). K312 a catalog of every measure with its explanation; K313 `app.language_view` (lines, lanes, shifts, setting changes).
- **U168** `GET /api/session/<id>/language`, cached by record size; the generator writes `web/vm/language_measures.gen.ts` from the catalog. **U169–U171** the language lens in meta, after structure: bars, deviation and ribbons views of the 13 watched measures per turn, setting changes as rules, shifts as ▲/▼, a readout, ↑↓←→↵. **U172** the bar line names its settings (`guidance high · length normal · tone neutral`) and shows shifts since the last change after their setting; `/language [open|clear|off|on]`; open shows the lens at the latest shift's reply.
- **Found in U172.** The lens's key handler read the Enter that runs `/language` as "open the stream", because the input bar had unmounted and `document.activeElement` no longer named it; it now judges by the key's target. The UI boundary test had failed since U168 (`gen_kinds.py` imported `substrate.language.*`); the kernel now exports `language_catalog` and `language_drift` from `substrate.app` (`aab8d900`). The full gate run caught a race: an earlier language fetch could land after a later one; `loadLanguage` now applies only the latest.
- **Design**: `process/planning/DESIGN-2026-10-13-language-in-the-app-r2.md`, the design sheet in `process/planning/design-sheets/2026-10-13-language/`, roadmap `ROADMAP-2026-10-13-language-in-the-app.md`.

It answers three questions: the field's theory of document organization (archival provenance, original order, lifecycle and continuum; faceted classification; Diátaxis and Google's documentation chapter; ADR, PEP 1 and RFC 2026 supersession; logs and lab notebooks; Lethbridge 2003, Parnas and Clements 1986), each source fetched and read on 2026-10-29 and listed with what was verified; the theory this project works by today, measured (four round markers, five ways of showing status, 12 sprint-card status values, five history homes, two sprint folders); and a plan of eight steps for these documents. No file has moved.

- **2026-10-29 user** — The documents are text, read mostly by model sessions and also by people; the aim is correctness, not the habits of the average engineer. Applied in `process/planning/open/PLAN-2026-10-29-document-organization-r2.md`: studies of how engineers maintain documents are no longer grounds; the rules rest on what a model reader can and cannot know (only its context; it cannot tell which of two documents is current; Liu et al., "Lost in the Middle", arXiv 2307.03172). New rules: a session reads the current set by default (`planning/open/`, the ground files, the tail of the records) and opens history only when a current document points to it; the records are read by command (`tail`, a decisions grep), not whole.

Disposition: the eight are being undone and checked against `baseline-2026-11-04`, the pre-move captures; the fixes logged above stay as logged; the rule is recorded in the agent's memory (a refactor changes nothing, bugs included; a bug found is logged, not fixed inside it) and supersedes plan round 5 wherever they differ. - **2026-11-05 agent-resolved** — How an event reaches a paused session's record without starting a turn (0(d)). - **2026-11-06 agent-resolved** — Where a server trace loss is reported. In sprint 353 the server reported it only in `server.jsonl`, on its next good write; a capped or unopenable file has none, so the loss reached no trace file. Decision: the server reports each loss burst on stdout (`substrate-ui trace_lost=<total>`: first loss at once, the rest on the next good write and at shutdown); main writes the difference to `main.jsonl` as `TRACE_LOST` (process server) and counts it in `APP_QUIT_COMPLETED.signals_lost`; the server writes no `TRACE_LOST` of its own, so each loss is reported once. `SUBSTRATE_SERVER_TRACE_CAP` caps `server.jsonl` for the loss gate; `SUBSTRATE_TRACE_CAP` caps main's files. Grounds: project — the window's losses already reach `main.jsonl` the same way (sprint 351); SDD — AGENTS.md "a failed append never stays silent" (plan item 2); practice — a process reports a failure on a channel other than the one that failed.
