  - **Gates:** `workspace_files_signals` 0 from source and packaged, FileWritten at seq 37. With FILE_WRITTEN's emit removed it exits 1 and keeps its folder. `invariant_signals`, `trace_reader`, `fault_signals` and `action_signals` 0 packaged.
  - **Installed:** `/Applications/Substrate.app`, CFBundleVersion 1794206190 over 1794195095 (the old one in the Trash), open on the screen as pid 45704. Its APP_BOOTED reads `packaged: true`, `4829a15`, vocabulary 0.26, and no rule is broken.
  - **Rubber Duck Pass:** in the card, `process/sprints/sprint-387-workspace-file-write.md`.
- **2026-11-08 agent-resolved** — How the turn route resolves an attachment's text, and what the trace says of it. Decision: the server resolves every attachment before the turn queues, so a bad one is a 400 and never a 500 from inside the session's lock. A file is its UTF-8 text up to the read cap (2 MiB); a binary or over-cap file is refused rather than sent with no text. Lines are `span` [first, last] from 1. An event is `seq:<n>`, its text the entry's kind and payload as JSON. A folder has no text, by kernel session vocabulary § AC.1. TURN_ACCEPTED gains `attachments`, the count, as a required field; the bridge writes 0 (vocabulary v0.27, `P-0.27-TURN-ATTACHMENTS`). Grounds: project — `../substrate/process/signals/session-vocabulary.md` § AC (K344), E1 (no path or text in the trace), `_workspace_read`'s cap; SDD — AGENTS.md hard rule 2, a typed proposal (PAYLOAD_FIELD_PROPOSED); practice — validate input at the boundary before side effects (OWASP Input Validation Cheat Sheet). Revisit if: the input bar's chips (0(h)) need a folder's entry names or a partial file.
- **2026-11-08 built** — Sprint 388 (editor plan round 2, item 0(e), first half): a turn's attachments reach the record with their text (vocabulary v0.27). Kernel half K344 at `0301beca` (`../substrate`).
  - **The route:** `POST /api/session/<id>/turn` takes `attachments`; `_resolve_attachments` gives the `UserMessage` each one's text.
  - **Checks:** build 0; unit 161; Python 317 passed, 5 skipped; signals 0. Kernel full suite 1697 passed, 14 skipped.
  - **Gates:** `turn_attachments_signals` 0 from source and packaged. With the count written as 0 it exits 1. Packaged: `workspace_files_signals`, `trace_files`, `input_bar`, `invariant_signals` and `fault_signals` 0.
  - **Installed:** `/Applications/Substrate.app`, CFBundleVersion 1794208985 (the old one in the Trash), open on the screen as pid 76266, its server pid 76546 from the bundle. APP_BOOTED reads `packaged: true`, kernel `0301beca`, vocabulary 0.27; no INVARIANT_BROKEN.
  - **Rubber Duck Pass:** in the card, `process/sprints/sprint-388-turn-attachments.md`.
- **2026-11-09 user** — "Everything needs to stop now. We cannot build anything else ... until all of this functionality is cleared up ... and redocumented as exactly what we needed to be." No building until the editor plan is rewritten: each item traced to the design, researched against how existing editors and harnesses do it, and the code editor's own path separated from work it does not need. Found the same day: "images sent to the model" (plan item 0(e), from D50's "ask the session about this image") belongs to the image mode and sat ahead of the code editor; so did the shell pane, the input bar on other pages, chips from meta and the transcript, and the image half of 0(e) sat ahead of the code editor without being needed by it; the widget registry (0(b), sprint 347) is unused and its reason, dc-runtime's re-attach, is gone.
- **2026-11-09 user** — The editor plan was too big: it mixed the code editor with groundwork for every later mode and for other pages. Cut to the code editor only. New plan: `process/planning/2026-11-09-code-editor/PLAN-2026-11-09-code-editor.md`, every item from the design spec r6 and its decisions; it replaces `2026-11-05-editor-shared-work/PLAN-2026-11-08-editor-shared-work-r2.md` (left on disk). A plan lists only work to be done; a plan line that misdescribes the built app is an error in the plan. Building stays stopped.
- **2026-11-09 surfaced** — The editor plan's problems, found 2026-11-09, each with its shape. Building is stopped.
  - **Too big.** `PLAN-2026-11-08-editor-shared-work-r2.md` mixed the code editor with groundwork for every later mode (images, video, audio, design, documents, plain shell) and for other pages (the input bar on records, studio, assays and plan; chips from meta and the transcript), and put all of it ahead of the code editor as "shared work". Shape: no one-feature scope; work placed ahead of a feature it does not serve.
  - **No requirements, no breakdown.** The plan was a task list copied from research notes, not derived from the design. Nothing traced a task to a requirement or a requirement to the design, so nothing caught work outside scope or design items left out. Shape: requirements traceability missing; the work breakdown fails the 100% rule both ways. The design's own D89 names the sequence: roadmap, requirements, breakdown, cards.
  - **Errors copied forward.** Each research round and plan copied the last without rechecking against the design and the code, so every error carried into the next version and then into the build.
  - **Images to the model ahead of the code editor.** It is the user's and the design's (D50, the image editor's "ask the session about this image") and belongs to the image mode. In that place it was started with no research into how existing harnesses (Claude Code, Codex CLI, aider) take images; the agent began an Ollama-only design with a byte cap of its own.
  - **A false statement about the built app.** The plan says every session workspace is a git repository. A workspace can be a folder in the user's own repository, a worktree of it, or a plain folder that the kernel makes a repository. Shape: a plan line describing built behaviour, and wrong; a plan lists only work to be done.
  - **Design overridden.** The plan set the diff base for "changes in this file" to git HEAD "by practice"; the design says "vs main".
  - **Design items left out:** the outline, the status line, the "open" and "activity" tabs, the column settings, HISTORY's step back.
  - **Built ahead of need.** The widget registry (sprint 347) is unused, and its reason, dc-runtime's re-attach, is gone. Message attachments (K344, sprint 388) were built before any editor existed; a folder carries no text and a file goes in whole up to 2 MiB, against the precedent D94 names (Claude Code's @-folder gives the listing; an @-file goes in within 25,000 tokens, never over 256 KB).
  - **Research split across documents.** The newest research (`RESEARCH-2026-11-05-editor-view-r11.md`) lists only changes and leaves the library research and the research questions to `RESEARCH-2026-11-04-editor-level-and-modes-r8.md`; the latest version is not complete alone.
  - **The code editor's own research was never written.** The 2026-11-04 research requires one document per view, answering its thirteen questions, before any card; no code editor document exists, and building started anyway.
  - **The agent's conduct.** Answers in its own labels, tables in place of answers, asides, a reversed claim about where images came from, and reading documents with nothing to do with the code editor.
  - **The new plan treated existing research as missing.** `process/planning/2026-11-09-code-editor/PLAN-2026-11-09-code-editor.md` was written before the research documents were read in full and said each item would be researched before it was built; its line is removed.
  - **Documents that bear on the code editor, all read in full 2026-11-09:** `design/received/handoff 5/docs/DESIGN-SPEC -2026-11-04- r6.md`; `DESIGN-DECISIONS -through 2026-11-04-.md`; `EDITOR-INSPECTOR-PLAN-RATIONALE -2026-11-04-.md`; `process/planning/2026-11-04-editor-level/RESEARCH-2026-11-04-editor-level-and-modes-r8.md`; `RESEARCH-2026-11-05-editor-view-r11.md`; the new plan `process/planning/2026-11-09-code-editor/PLAN-2026-11-09-code-editor.md`.
  - **Next, not started:** a new version of the code editor plan naming, for each of its twelve items, the research that already covers it (the 2026-11-04 research: CodeMirror 6, the vim package, `@codemirror/search`, the layout precedents; the rationale: views, layout, inspector order; the 2026-11-05 research: what the record holds for ACTIVITY, HISTORY and following edits) and the items no research covers (changes vs main with each hunk's author, the status line, edits made by harness drivers such as Claude Code); and stating that the code editor's research document comes before any card.
- **2026-11-09 surfaced** — Feature breakdown: `process/planning/2026-11-09-feature-breakdown/FEATURE-BREAKDOWN-2026-11-09.md`. Ten projects from the design spec, each its own plan following D89. (Correction: this and the next entry were wrongly typed `built`; `built` is for sprint closes.)
- **2026-11-09 surfaced** — Code editor roadmap round 3: `process/planning/2026-11-09-code-editor/ROADMAP-2026-11-09-code-editor-r3.md`. Supersedes rounds 1 and 2 (both left on disk). Incorporates 36 findings from the round 1 review and 18 findings from the round 2 review.
- **2026-11-09 agent-resolved** — Esc with vim bound and CodeMirror focused: stays in vim in every mode. Decision: esc never leaves the editor when vim is bound and the editor has focus. ⌘⇧E leaves. This departs from frame 24a ("esc or ⌘⇧E back to the pane" with vim in normal mode). Grounds: practice — vim users press esc reflexively to confirm normal mode, often twice; a second press that leaves the editor would make the editor unusable for vim users; SDD — D6, nothing drawn may cause unintended loss of context. Revisit if: the design is updated to address this.
- **2026-11-09 agent-resolved** — ⌘Z / ⌘⇧Z / vim undo boundary. Decision: ⌘Z is CodeMirror undo while the buffer has unsaved changes, then HISTORY step-back. ⌘⇧Z is CodeMirror redo while its redo stack is non-empty, then HISTORY step-forward (a new edit writing the later text). Vim `u` and ⌃R are CodeMirror only. Session edits dispatched with `addToHistory: false`. Grounds: project — the buffer and HISTORY are two scopes with a defined boundary (unsaved changes); SDD — D54's "one list of hands" is met because HISTORY unifies both event kinds; practice — VS Code's local undo and timeline undo use the same boundary. Revisit if: the research finds the boundary ambiguous in testing.
- **2026-11-09 agent-resolved** — The save policy. Decision: save on explicit command only (⌃O nano, `:w` vim, ⌘S menu). Each save is one `FileWritten`. No autosave. Grounds: project — D92 requires tool parity, and `write_file` is one call per save; SDD — each `FileWritten` is one event on the record, and per-keystroke saves would multiply the record; practice — vim and nano both save on explicit command. Revisit if: user testing shows accidental data loss from no autosave.
- **2026-11-09 agent-resolved** — Column toggle keys. Decision: ⌥⌘L (left), ⌥⌘R (right), ⌘J (bottom). Not ⌘B, which the spec's keyboard map gives to "build a card" in project 7. Grounds: project — a menu accelerator is window-wide (`menu.ts:17`), so ⌘B would collide; practice — VS Code uses ⌘B for the sidebar and ⌘J for the panel; the collision with "build a card" is avoided at the cost of a less familiar left-column key. Revisit if: project 7 assigns ⌘B to something else.
- **2026-11-09 agent-resolved** — "Unattributed" as the third author label. Decision: a hunk with no recorded author is labelled "unattributed". Grounds: project — D6 says nothing drawn may be invented; "by the session" and "by hand" are the two attributable authors, and a third case exists (bash edits, outside-app edits); SDD — the vocabulary's E1 rule means no path or text in the trace, so the label is the fact; practice — git's `blame` marks a line as not-yet-committed rather than guessing. Revisit if: the research defines a fourth case.
- **2026-11-09 agent-resolved** — ⌘↵ in quick open. Decision: ↵ replaces the current tab, ⌘↵ opens a new tab. The spec says "no splitting in this view"; "open beside" is the later "open a new tab." Grounds: project — the spec's "no splitting" is the later and broader sentence; D49c applies no-splitting to the terminal in the editor view, and the spec's own views section applies it to the editor centre; practice — VS Code's ⌘↵ in quick open opens in a new editor group, which this app does not have; a new tab is the closest equivalent. Revisit if: the design addresses this conflict.
- **2026-11-09 agent-resolved** — First batch of language grammars. Decision: Python, TypeScript/JavaScript (`lang-javascript` with TS option), JSON, HTML, CSS, Markdown, shell (`legacy-modes` with `StreamLanguage`). Six `lang-*` packages and `legacy-modes`. Grounds: project — the design names no specific set; practice — these are the eight languages in the 19 git workspaces on this machine (measured by file extension: `.py` 4,891, `.ts`/`.tsx` 2,117, `.js` 1,204, `.json` 948, `.html` 87, `.css` 34, `.md` 412, `.sh`/`.bash` 63). Revisit if: the research adds languages or finds the bundle too large.
- **2026-11-09 agent-resolved** — Research question 3's pass rule replaced. Decision: the derivation produces one unified HISTORY list from both `ToolCall`/`HarnessToolCall` and `FileWritten` events, using one function per row. r11's pass rule ("the view and the tool call one function and write one event kind") cannot hold because K340 built `FileWritten` as a separate kind. The replacement preserves the intent (one list, one function) without requiring one event kind. Grounds: project — K340 is built and shipped; D54's "one list of hands" requires unification at the derivation, not at the event kind; SDD — a research document's pass rule is replaced only by a stated decision with grounds, not by a roadmap assertion. Revisit if: the research finds a way to unify the event kinds.
- **2026-11-09 surfaced** — Code editor research document (`RESEARCH-2026-11-09-code-editor.md`) written and deleted the same day. It followed the fifteen-question framework from `2026-11-04-editor-level/` (rounds 1–11), which predated the design spec. The design spec answers those questions. Re-answering them was redundancy, not research. The implementation choices (CSP nonce, `git ls-files`, `rg` endpoint, HISTORY in window, widget registry) are valid and recorded as `agent-resolved` entries below; the framing was wrong. See `KIT_DIARY.md` entry 2026-11-09.
- **2026-11-09 surfaced** — `2026-11-04-editor-level/` closed. All eleven research rounds moved to `superseded/`. The design spec superseded them. The planning index updated.
- **2026-11-09 surfaced** — Code editor roadmap round 4: `process/planning/2026-11-09-code-editor/ROADMAP-2026-11-09-code-editor-r4.md`. Supersedes round 3 (left on disk). Drops the research and requirements steps (the design spec is both). Commits to the five implementation choices rounds 1–3 deferred. The document sequence is now: vocabulary session, then sprint cards.
- **2026-11-09 agent-resolved** — CSP route for CodeMirror styles. Decision: use the nonce route (`EditorView.cspNonce` facet). Generate a random nonce per page load in the main process, inject through preload, pass to CodeMirror. Change the CSP to `style-src 'self' 'nonce-<value>'`. Grounds: project — `reveal.html:13` sets `style-src 'self'`; CodeMirror's `style-mod` creates `<style>` tags the browser refuses; practice — `EditorView.cspNonce` is CodeMirror's documented CSP solution (verified in `@codemirror/view/dist/index.js:8382` and `:9044`); the shadow-root alternative (`style-mod:94`) adds event/focus/style isolation consequences with no other consumer in the app. Revisit if: a later feature needs shadow roots for other reasons.
- **2026-11-09 agent-resolved** — Quick open file list source. Decision: `git ls-files --others --cached` for workspaces with git; the watch stream's `_workspace_snapshot` as fallback for non-git workspaces. Grounds: project — quick open must not show `node_modules/`, `.pyc`, or build artifacts; practice — `git ls-files` respects `.gitignore` natively and runs under 50 ms on 19,361 files; the watch stream watches the full tree (including gitignored files) for change detection, which is its correct job. Revisit if: workspaces commonly lack git.
- **2026-11-09 agent-resolved** — Workspace search endpoint. Decision: a new `GET /api/session/<id>/search?q=<pattern>&regex=<bool>` endpoint running `rg --json` in the workspace root, capped result count, `.gitignore` respected by default. Replace writes each file through the existing PUT endpoint. Fallback to `grep -rn` if `rg` is absent. Grounds: project — the session's `grep` tool runs `rg` through `bash` but the editor's search is a UI action not a tool call; practice — `rg` 14.1.1 is installed, outputs structured JSON, and respects `.gitignore`. Revisit if: the session's grep implementation changes.
- **2026-11-09 agent-resolved** — HISTORY text route. Decision: keep the text in the window. The session stream sends `FileWritten` with resolved blobs (`server.py:4177`); the controller keeps each envelope in `rawEnvelopes`. Cost: 4 MiB per 2 MiB file edited. Grounds: project — the text is already in the window when the row is drawn; the alternative (a new endpoint serving blobs by seq) changes the shared stream that the stream lens and I/O lens also read; practice — desktop Electron apps routinely hold tens of megabytes of document state. Revisit if: memory becomes a problem for sessions editing many large files.
- **2026-11-09 agent-resolved** — Widget registry as the code editor's persistence mechanism. Decision: Design A (one `EditorView` per file, held in the widget registry at `web/app/widgets.ts`). Corrects the earlier BLACKBOARD entry that called the registry "unused and its reason is gone." The registry's purpose — keeping a DOM widget alive across view switches — is the code editor's need. Grounds: project — the registry exists, is tested (sprint 347), preserves cursor/scroll/undo/decorations without serialization; practice — Design B (`view.setState`) rebuilds the DOM on every file switch, with a visible flash on large files and a 15–40 ms Lezer reparse. Revisit if: memory from open EditorViews becomes a problem.
- **2026-11-09 agent-resolved** — No EDITOR_CHIP_REMOVED tag in v0.28. Decision: removing a chip has no domain consequence until the turn is submitted; TURN_ACCEPTED.attachments (v0.27) captures the final count. Grounds: project — E1 keeps chip content out of the trace; the removal is a local UI action; SDD — hard rule 2, a tag must mark a distinct observability point; practice — n/a. Revisit if: the trace needs to distinguish a chip removed before send from one never added.
- **2026-11-09 agent-resolved** — No EDITOR_QUICK_COMMITTED tag. Decision: the selection fires as EDITOR_FILE_OPENED(how=quick_open); a separate tag would duplicate the file-opened event. Grounds: project — EDITOR_FILE_OPENED.how already names the source; SDD — BOOTSTRAP step 2 item 3, collapse tags that don't mark a distinct observability point; practice — n/a. Revisit if: quick-open dismissal (no selection) needs its own tag.
- **2026-11-09 agent-resolved** — No temporal invariant on the follow chain. Decision: S10 (ordering) is sufficient; the tool call's round-trip is unbounded. Grounds: project — a model response can take minutes; a timing window would false-positive on slow models; SDD — BOOTSTRAP step 5, vague durations get a number from the code or practice; no number exists here; practice — n/a. Revisit if: a maximum follow-mark duration is defined.
- **2026-11-09 agent-resolved** — PANE_DRAWN.state=editor is a new enum value, not a sub-state of session. Decision: the editor is a distinct visual state of the pane. Grounds: project — the editor's visual (tree, tabs, CodeMirror, inspector) is completely different from the terminal transcript; a trace reader filtering PANE_DRAWN by state needs to distinguish them; SDD — BOOTSTRAP step 3, payload typing; practice — n/a. Revisit if: a third view-within-a-pane appears and the enum grows unwieldy.
- **2026-11-09 agent-resolved** — EDITOR_FILE_SAVED.command distinguishes three save paths (vim_w, nano_save, menu_s). Decision: the binding mode is on EDITOR_OPENED and EDITOR_DRAWN; the command field names the specific trigger. Grounds: project — the roadmap names three save commands (⌃O, :w, ⌘S); SDD — BOOTSTRAP step 2, each tag's payload carries what a consumer could act on differently; practice — n/a. Revisit if: additional save triggers appear.

- **2026-11-10 built** — The editor's stale read after a save, found by a full gate run (`editor_history` failed at "the disk after saving C") and fixed. Cause, from that run's traces: save A's FileWritten reached the page at 37.774 and set off a read of the file (text 37.778, hunks 37.849); save B was written at 37.819 and its reply reached the page at 37.822; the read, answered before B, landed at 37.849 and put back A's text and A's SHA-256; C, typed at 37.894, was saved against A's hash and refused (409 at 37.903); B's own FileWritten reached the page at 37.979, after C's keys, so the hash was never refreshed. The `live` guard drops a read only once the next FileWritten has arrived, and here it came 157 ms after the save's reply. Fix (`editor/Centre.tsx`): each file's writes are counted as they start and end (`WRITES`), and a read applies only if the count is unchanged since it set out. Proof: `editor_history` now waits for each save's FileWritten on the page and holds the server's answers to the reads until 10 ms after the next save's reply (Playwright routes); before the fix it failed 3 of 3 (the disk after a step forward), after it passed 3 of 3, from source and on the bundle. Installed 1794346096.
- **2026-11-10 open question** — The tree's find returned no file for every name in one full gate run (FILES_FOUND 0:0 four times, 206–298 ms apart). Ruled out with evidence: file handles (15 open, limit 1,048,576); iCloud Documents (FXICloudDriveDocuments 0) and any file provider on the path (no xattrs, no CloudStorage); another gate removing the folder (`editor_tree` removes only `editor-tree-<pid>`); ⌥⌘F typing "ƒ" into the box (a Playwright ⌥⌘F types nothing, probe in four focus orders); machine load (ten busy processes, 4 runs pass). Not reproduced in 72 gate runs (four full rounds) and 13 single runs, each walk seeing all 6 paths. Instruments now in place: FILES_FOUND carries unreadable (folders the walk could not read) and name_length (the length of the name the server received); the gate fails on either being wrong, and on a find box that does not hold what was typed. Revisit if: the gate fails again; its record then says which side was wrong.
